<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empty cart careened down the hill and struck a fence.  空车摇晃着冲下山坡，撞上了篱笆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dodder</td><td>/ˈdɑːdər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>蹒跚，颤巍巍地行进</td><td>dodder along the path<br>沿小路蹒跚而行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man doddered along the path with a cane in each hand.  老人两手各持一杖，沿小路蹒跚而行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 falter</td><td>/ˈfɔːltər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>蹒跚；犹豫，踌躇</td><td>falter in his resolve<br>决心动摇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mr. Garrison never faltered in his resolve to finish the project.  加里森先生完成这个项目的决心从未动摇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reel</td><td>/riːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>感到眩晕</td><td>reel from the blow<br>被打得头昏眼花</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boxer reeled from the blow and clutched at the ropes.  拳手被那一击打得头昏眼花，抓住了围绳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stagger</td><td>/ˈstæɡər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>摇晃地走，蹒跚</td><td>stagger to his feet<br>摇摇晃晃站起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exhausted runner staggered across the finish line.  疲惫的跑者摇摇晃晃冲过终点线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stumble</td><td>/ˈstʌmbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>绊倒，跌跌撞撞</td><td>stumble over a root<br>被树根绊倒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stumbled on the dark staircase but did not fall.  她在漆黑的楼梯上绊了一下，但没有摔倒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 teeter</td><td>/ˈtiːtər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇晃，摇摇欲坠</td><td>teeter on the edge<br>在边缘摇摇欲坠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company teetered on the edge of bankruptcy for several months.  公司数月来一直在破产边缘摇摇欲坠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 totter</td><td>/ˈtɑːtər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇摇晃晃地走，蹒跚</td><td>totter across the room<br>摇晃着走过房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The toddler tottered a few steps and then sat down.  幼儿摇摇晃晃走了几步就坐下了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waddle</td><td>/ˈwɑːdl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇摇摆摆地走</td><td>waddle like a duck<br>像鸭子般摇摆走</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fat puppy waddled across the kitchen toward its water bowl.  那只胖胖的小狗摇摇摆摆地穿过厨房走向水碗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 progress smoothly</td><td>/prəˈɡres ˈsmuːðli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>顺利进行，平稳推进</td><td>progress smoothly toward completion<br>顺利推进直到完成</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Talks progressed smoothly once both sides dropped their preconditions.  双方放弃前提条件后，谈判进展顺利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>lurk</b></td><td>/lɜːrk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>潜伏</td><td>lurk in the shadows<br>潜伏在暗处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A photographer lurked in the shadows outside the actress's hotel all night.  一名摄影师整夜潜伏在女演员酒店外的暗处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ambush</td><td>/ˈæmˌbʊʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>埋伏，伏击</td><td>lie in ambush<br>设伏等候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rebels ambushed the supply convoy in a narrow valley.  叛军在狭窄的山谷里伏击了补给车队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snake</td><td>/ˈsneɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>蜿蜒潜行；蛇</td><td>snake through the grass<br>在草丛中蜿蜒潜行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The narrow path snakes quietly along the base of the cliff.  狭窄的小路沿着崖底静静蜿蜒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 steal</td><td>/ˈstiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>潜行，悄悄移动；偷</td><td>steal into the room<br>悄悄溜进房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Each night he would steal quietly out of the house.  他每晚都会悄悄溜出房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appear</td><td>/əˈpɪr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>出现，显露</td><td>appear out of nowhere<br>不知从哪儿冒出来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A figure appeared at the far end of the dark corridor.  一个人影出现在黑暗走廊的另一端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 come out</td><td>/kʌm ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>出现，露面；显露</td><td>come out of hiding<br>从藏身处现身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stars come out over the desert as soon as clouds clear.  云一散开，星星就在沙漠上空出来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>lush</b></td><td>/lʌʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>茂盛的，繁茂的</td><td>lush vegetation<br>茂盛的植被</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lush vegetation covered the valley floor after weeks of steady rain.  连续数周的降雨之后，茂盛的植被覆盖了谷底。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cornucopian</del></td><td>/ˌkɔrnəˈkoʊpiən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，充裕的</td><td>cornucopian abundance<br>极为丰饶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market offered a cornucopian display of fruit.  市场上摆着丰富多样的水果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fecund</td><td>/ˈfiːkənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多产的，肥沃的</td><td>a fecund imagination<br>丰富的想象力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novelist had a fecund imagination and published a book yearly.  这位小说家想象力丰富多产，每年都出一本书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fruitful</td><td>/ˈfruːtfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>富有成果的，多产的</td><td>a fruitful discussion<br>富有成果的讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their collaboration proved fruitful and produced several important papers.  他们的合作富有成果，产出了几篇重要论文。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 productive</td><td>/prəˈdʌktɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多产的，富有成效的</td><td>a productive meeting<br>富有成效的会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The morning was productive; we finished three chapters before lunch.  上午很有成效，午饭前我们写完了三章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prolific</td><td>/prəˈlɪfɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多产的，大量的</td><td>a prolific writer<br>多产的作家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was a prolific painter who finished hundreds of canvases.  她是位多产的画家，完成了数百幅作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rich</td><td>/ˈrɪtʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>肥沃的，丰富的；富有的</td><td>rich farmland<br>肥沃的农田</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delta has rich soil ideal for growing rice.  三角洲土壤肥沃，非常适合种稻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 barren</td><td>/ˈbærən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无成果的</td><td>barren land<br>贫瘠的土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing grows on the barren land beyond the last village.  最后一个村子之外的贫瘠土地上什么都长不出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dead</td><td>/ˈded/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>死的；毫无生气的</td><td>a dead town at night<br>夜里死寂的小镇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The resort is completely dead in the winter months.  这个度假地在冬季几个月里毫无生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infertile</td><td>/ɪnˈfɜːrtəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；不育的</td><td>infertile ground<br>贫瘠的土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of drought left the ground almost completely infertile.  多年干旱使土地几乎完全贫瘠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sterile</td><td>/ˈsterəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无菌的</td><td>sterile medical instruments<br>无菌医疗器械</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Surgeons must use sterile instruments to prevent infection during any operation.  外科医生必须使用无菌器械，以防手术中感染。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unproductive</td><td>/ˌʌnprəˈdʌktɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无成效的</td><td>unproductive rocky soil<br>贫瘠的石质土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of drought left the fields dry and unproductive.  多年干旱使这些田地干枯贫瘠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>lustrous</b></td><td>/ˈlʌstrəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有光泽的</td><td>lustrous black hair<br>有光泽的黑发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lustrous black hair shone under the bright lights of the stage.  她有光泽的黑发在明亮的舞台灯光下闪耀。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brilliant</td><td>/ˈbrɪljənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪颖的；辉煌的</td><td>a brilliant student<br>才华出众的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was a brilliant mathematician long before she turned twenty.  她还没到二十岁就已是出色的数学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 burnished</td><td>/ˈbɜːrnɪʃt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>擦亮的，有光泽的</td><td>burnished copper<br>擦亮的铜器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The burnished brass handle shone brightly in the afternoon sunlight.  擦亮的铜把手在午后阳光下闪亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gleaming</td><td>/ˈɡliːmɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>闪亮的，光洁的</td><td>gleaming marble floors<br>光洁发亮的大理石地面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She polished the old silver teapot until it was gleaming.  她把那把旧银茶壶擦得闪闪发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glistening</td><td>/ˈɡlɪsənɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>闪光的，闪亮的</td><td>glistening wet streets<br>湿漉发亮的街道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Glistening drops of rain hung from every bare branch.  闪亮的雨珠挂在每一根光枝上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glossy</td><td>/ˈɡlɑːsi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平滑的，有光泽的</td><td>glossy hair<br>有光泽的头发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magazine printed her photograph on thick and glossy paper.  杂志把她的照片印在厚实而有光泽的纸上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 polished</td><td>/ˈpɑːlɪʃt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的</td><td>polished manners<br>优雅得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His polished manners impressed everyone at the formal dinner.  他优雅得体的举止让正式晚宴上的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 refulgent</del></td><td>/rɪˈfʌldʒənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>辉煌的，灿烂的</td><td>refulgent golden domes<br>灿烂的金色圆顶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refulgent golden domes rose above the rooftops of the old city.  灿烂的金色圆顶耸立在老城的屋顶之上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rubbed</td><td>/rʌbd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>擦亮的，磨光的</td><td>rubbed to a soft shine<br>擦出柔和的光泽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The table top was rubbed smooth by generations of hands.  桌面被几代人的手摩擦得光滑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shining</td><td>/ˈʃaɪnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>闪亮的，发光的</td><td>shining armor<br>闪亮的铠甲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her shining eyes showed how much the praise meant to her.  她发亮的眼睛显示出这份赞扬对她多重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sleek</td><td>/sliːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>时髦的，优美的</td><td>a sleek silver car<br>线条优美的银色轿车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She drove a sleek silver car that turned heads on every street.  她开着一辆线条优美的银色轿车，一路引人注目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 splendid</td><td>/ˈsplendɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>壮丽的；极好的</td><td>a splendid marble hall<br>壮丽的大理石厅堂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the roof we had a splendid view of the harbor.  从屋顶望去，港口的景色极为壮丽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dim</td><td>/ˈdɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>暗淡的，模糊的</td><td>a dim light<br>昏暗的灯光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was too dim for reading anything but large print.  房间太暗，只能看大字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dull</td><td>/dʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的；乏味的</td><td>a dull student<br>迟钝的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was so dull that half the class slept.  讲座乏味得让一半学生睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lackluster</td><td>/ˈlæklʌstər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>黯淡无光泽的</td><td>a lackluster performance<br>平淡无光的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lackluster hair and tired face worried everyone at home.  她黯淡的头发和疲惫的脸让家人都担心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 lusterless</del></td><td>/ˈlʌstərləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无光泽的，暗淡的</td><td>lusterless hair<br>无光泽的头发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old coins were lusterless but perfectly preserved.  这些旧硬币虽无光泽却保存完好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>luxurious</b></td><td>/lʌɡˈʒʊriəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>奢侈的</td><td>a luxurious hotel suite<br>豪华的酒店套房</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They spent their anniversary in a luxurious hotel suite overlooking the harbor.  他们在一间俯瞰港口的豪华酒店套房里度过了结婚纪念日。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decadent</td><td>/ˈdekədənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>堕落的，腐败的</td><td>a decadent ruling class<br>腐败堕落的统治阶层</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He described the court as decadent, idle, and blind to reform.  他形容那个宫廷腐败堕落、无所作为、对改革视而不见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indulgent</td><td>/ɪnˈdʌldʒənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（对己）放纵的，（对他人）纵容的</td><td>an indulgent parent<br>溺爱孩子的家长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her indulgent grandparents let her eat whatever she wanted.  溺爱她的祖父母让她想吃什么就吃什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 overindulgent</del></td><td>/ˌoʊvərɪnˈdʌldʒənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过分放纵的，过分溺爱的</td><td>overindulgent parents<br>过分溺爱的父母</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Overindulgent grandparents spoiled the boy with endless gifts.  过分溺爱的祖父母用无尽的礼物宠坏了这男孩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 self-indulgent</td><td>/ˌsɛlf ɪnˈdʌldʒənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>放纵自己的，纵情享乐的</td><td>a self-indulgent lifestyle<br>放纵享乐的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent a self-indulgent week eating out and sleeping late.  他放纵地过了一周，天天下馆子、睡到很晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 sybaritic</del></td><td>/ˌsɪbəˈrɪtɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>穷奢极欲的，享乐的</td><td>a sybaritic lifestyle<br>穷奢极欲的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The emperor's sybaritic banquets lasted from dusk until dawn.  皇帝穷奢极欲的宴会从黄昏持续到黎明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abstemious</td><td>/əbˈstiːmiəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（吃喝等）有节制的，节俭的</td><td>an abstemious diet<br>有节制的饮食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He led an abstemious life, eating little and drinking less.  他生活节制，吃得少喝得更少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abstinent</td><td>/ˈæbstənənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>节制的，禁欲的</td><td>remain abstinent from alcohol<br>戒酒不饮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed abstinent from meat and wine for ten years.  他十年间不吃肉不喝酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>lyric</b></td><td>/ˈlɪrɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>如诗歌般流畅甜美的</td><td>a lyric passage of prose<br>一段流畅优美的散文</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel contains a lyric passage of prose about summer rain.  这部小说中有一段关于夏雨的抒情散文。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 euphonious</td><td>/juːˈfoʊniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悦耳的</td><td>a euphonious name<br>悦耳的名字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet chose euphonious words that pleased the ear when read aloud.  诗人选用朗读时悦耳的词语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lyrical</td><td>/ˈlɪrɪkəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>抒情的；流畅优美的</td><td>a lyrical description of spring<br>对春天的抒情描写</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lyrical prose makes even a train ride sound beautiful.  她抒情的文笔让坐火车都显得很美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mellifluous</td><td>/meˈlɪfluəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（声音）甜美流畅的</td><td>a mellifluous voice<br>甜美悦耳的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The narrator's mellifluous voice made even the dullest chapters pleasant to listen to.  叙述者优美悦耳的嗓音让最枯燥的章节也变得动听。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mellow</td><td>/ˈmeloʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>温和的，圆熟的；变温和</td><td>grow mellow with age<br>随年岁渐趋温和</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has grown much mellower since he became a grandfather.  当上祖父后他变得温和多了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 melodious</td><td>/məˈloʊdiəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悦耳的，旋律优美的</td><td>a melodious voice<br>悦耳的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her melodious laugh carried across the courtyard on the still evening.  她悦耳的笑声在静夜里穿过庭院。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 musical</td><td>/ˈmjuːzɪkəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悦耳的，音调优美的</td><td>a soft musical voice<br>轻柔悦耳的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He had a low musical laugh that everyone remembered.  他有一种低沉悦耳的笑声，人人都记得。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 poetical</td><td>/poʊˈetɪkəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>富有诗意的；诗歌的</td><td>oddly poetical language<br>奇特而富诗意的语言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old letter was written in oddly poetical language.  那封旧信用了一种奇特而富诗意的语言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prosaic</td><td>/prəˈzeɪɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调的，常见的</td><td>prosaic details<br>平淡的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Behind the legend lies a prosaic story about a lost receipt.  传说背后是一个关于遗失收据的平淡故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prose</td><td>/ˈproʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>散文；平淡的文字</td><td>clear simple prose<br>清晰朴素的散文</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He writes plain prose rather than flowery verse.  他写朴实的散文，而不是华丽的诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>macabre</b></td><td>/məˈkɑːbrə/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恐怖的</td><td>a macabre tale<br>恐怖的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He entertained the children with a macabre tale about a haunted mill.  他用一个关于鬼屋磨坊的恐怖故事逗孩子们开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appalling</td><td>/əˈpɔːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>骇人的，糟糕透顶的</td><td>appalling living conditions<br>骇人的居住条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers lived in appalling conditions close to the mine.  工人们住在矿区附近骇人的条件中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 atrocious</td><td>/əˈtroʊʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极坏的，极其残忍的</td><td>an atrocious crime<br>残暴的罪行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the killing as an atrocious crime.  法庭称这起杀人事件是极其残忍的罪行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dreadful</td><td>/ˈdredfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，糟透的</td><td>a dreadful mistake<br>可怕的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a dreadful headache and went home early.  她头疼得厉害，很早就回家了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ghastly</td><td>/ˈɡæstli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的；惨白的</td><td>a ghastly discovery<br>可怕的发现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He turned a ghastly white when he heard the news.  他听到消息时脸色惨白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gruesome</td><td>/ˈɡruːsəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，令人毛骨悚然的</td><td>gruesome details<br>可怕的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness described the gruesome scene in a shaking voice.  证人用颤抖的声音描述了那可怕的场面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hideous</td><td>/ˈhɪdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常丑陋的</td><td>a hideous mask<br>丑陋可怕的面具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tore down the hideous concrete tower that blocked the view.  他们拆掉了那座挡住视野的丑陋混凝土塔楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 horrific</td><td>/hɔˈrɪfɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，令人恐惧的</td><td>a horrific traffic accident<br>可怕的交通事故</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Survivors described the horrific scene inside the burning tunnel.  幸存者描述了燃烧隧道里可怕的场面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nightmarish</td><td>/ˈnaɪtˌmerɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>如噩梦般恐怖的</td><td>a nightmarish journey home<br>噩梦般的回家路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Crossing the flooded streets was a nightmarish experience.  穿过被水淹的街道是噩梦般的经历。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 terrific</td><td>/təˈrɪfɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极好的，了不起的；极大的</td><td>a terrific new job<br>一份很棒的新工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You did a terrific job on that presentation yesterday.  你昨天那个演讲做得非常棒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 agreeable</td><td>/əˈɡriːəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉悦的；相一致的</td><td>an agreeable companion<br>一个讨人喜欢的同伴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found him an agreeable companion on the long train journey.  她觉得他是长途火车旅程中一位讨人喜欢的同伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 delightful</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪtfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的，宜人的</td><td>a delightful surprise<br>令人愉快的惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a delightful evening by the lake.  我们在湖边度过了一个愉快的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enjoyable</td><td>/ɪnˈdʒɔɪəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的，有趣的</td><td>an enjoyable evening<br>愉快的一晚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We had a thoroughly enjoyable evening at the concert.  我们在音乐会上度过了非常愉快的一晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pleasant</td><td>/ˈplezənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的；和善的</td><td>a pleasant surprise<br>令人愉快的惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a pleasant afternoon walking along the quiet river.  我们沿着静静的河边散步，度过了愉快的下午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>macerate</b></td><td>/ˈmæsəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>浸泡（以软化）</td><td>macerate fruit in wine<br>用酒浸软水果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chef macerated the fruit in sweet wine for several hours.  厨师把水果在甜酒中浸软了好几个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drench</td><td>/drentʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使湿透，浸透</td><td>drenched to the skin<br>浑身湿透</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sudden storm drenched us to the skin before we reached shelter.  一场突来的暴雨在我们找到避处前就把我们浇得浑身湿透。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drown</td><td>/ˈdraʊn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>溺死；淹没</td><td>drown out the noise<br>把噪音压过去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud applause drowned out the speaker's closing words completely.  响亮的掌声完全淹没了演讲者的结束语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impregnate</td><td>/ɪmˈpreɡneɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使浸透，使充满；使受孕</td><td>impregnate cloth with oil<br>使布浸透油</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wood is impregnated with resin so that it resists water.  这种木材浸透了树脂，因此能防水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 saturate</td><td>/ˈsætʃəreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使饱和，浸透</td><td>saturate the sponge<br>使海绵吸饱水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy rain had saturated the field before the match even began.  比赛还没开始，大雨已浸透了球场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sodden</td><td>/ˈsɑːdn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>湿透的</td><td>sodden ground<br>湿透的地面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our boots sank into the sodden ground after three days of rain.  连下三天雨后，我们的靴子陷进湿透的地里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sop</td><td>/sɑːp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>浸湿；吸干（液体）；安慰物</td><td>sop up the spilled milk<br>吸干洒出的牛奶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used a towel to sop up the milk spilled on the floor.  他用毛巾吸干洒在地上的牛奶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 souse</td><td>/saʊs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>浸泡；浇透</td><td>souse herring in brine<br>把鲱鱼泡在盐水里</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She soused the vegetables in cold water before cooking them.  她做菜前把蔬菜泡在冷水里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 steep</td><td>/stiːp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>浸泡，浸渍</td><td>steep tea leaves in hot water<br>把茶叶泡在热水里</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Steep the dried leaves in boiling water for about ten minutes.  把干叶子在沸水里浸泡约十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wring</td><td>/ˈrɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拧，绞；强行取得</td><td>wring a confession from him<br>从他口中逼出供认</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer wrung an admission from the reluctant witness.  律师从不情愿的证人口中硬逼出一句承认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>maculate</b></del></td><td>/ˈmækjəleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使有斑点，弄脏；玷污，损坏</td><td>maculate the white cloth<br>把白布弄上污斑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Careless painters maculated the white cloth with drops of red paint.  粗心的漆工用红漆点污了那块白布。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 besmirch</td><td>/bɪˈsmɜːrtʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诽谤，玷污</td><td>besmirch his reputation<br>诽谤他的名誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sued the paper for trying to besmirch his reputation.  他起诉该报意图诽谤他的名誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dot</td><td>/ˈdɑːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使布满小点；小圆点</td><td>dot the map with pins<br>在地图上插满小旗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Small green islands dot the blue bay near the harbor.  小小的绿岛点缀在港口附近蔚蓝的海湾上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dirty</td><td>/ˈdɜːrti/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>肮脏的；卑鄙的</td><td>dirty windows<br>脏污的窗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children came home with dirty hands and happy faces.  孩子们回家时手很脏，脸上却很开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 soil</td><td>/ˈsɔɪl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>弄脏，玷污；土壤</td><td>soil his good name<br>玷污他的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One reckless lie soiled a reputation built over many years.  一个轻率的谎言玷污了多年建立的声誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spot</td><td>/ˈspɑːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>污点，斑点；地点</td><td>a spot on the shirt<br>衬衫上的污点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A grease spot on the sleeve ruined his only white shirt.  袖子上的油污弄坏了他唯一的白衬衫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stain</td><td>/ˈsteɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>沾污，玷污；污点</td><td>stain his reputation<br>玷污他的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scandal stained the family name for two whole generations.  这桩丑闻让这个家族的名声蒙污两代人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clean</td><td>/ˈkliːn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>干净的；打扫，清洗</td><td>a clean white shirt<br>干净的白衬衫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Keep the wound clean and change the bandage every day.  保持伤口干净，每天更换绷带。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cleanse</td><td>/ˈklenz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>清洗，净化</td><td>cleanse the wound carefully<br>仔细清洗伤口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She cleansed the deep cut with warm water and soap.  她用温水和肥皂清洗了那道深口子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 purify</td><td>/ˈpjʊrəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>净化，使纯净</td><td>purify the water<br>净化水质</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These tablets purify water so it becomes safe to drink.  这些药片能净化水，使其可安全饮用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wash</td><td>/ˈwɑːʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>冲洗，洗涤</td><td>wash away the dirt<br>冲掉污垢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy rain washed away the dirt from the steps.  大雨把台阶上的污垢冲掉了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>maelstrom</b></td><td>/ˈmeɪlstrəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱、动荡的局势</td><td>a maelstrom of emotions<br>情绪的漩涡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young widow was caught in a maelstrom of grief and anger.  这位年轻的寡妇陷入了悲伤与愤怒的漩涡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chaos</td><td>/ˈkeɪɑːs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，无秩序</td><td>descend into chaos<br>陷入混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The power cut threw the whole airport into chaos.  停电让整个机场陷入混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disorder</td><td>/dɪˈsɔːrdər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>混乱，无序；失调，疾病</td><td>public disorder<br>公共秩序混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was left in complete disorder after the party.  聚会之后房间被弄得一片混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pandemonium</td><td>/ˌpændɪˈmoʊniəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，一片混乱</td><td>pandemonium broke out<br>顿时一片混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pandemonium broke out when the final goal was scored.  最后一个进球时全场一片沸腾混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tumult</td><td>/ˈtuːmʌlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骚动，暴动</td><td>the tumult of the crowd<br>人群的骚动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tumult in the square grew louder as more protesters arrived.  随着更多抗议者到来，广场上的骚动越来越大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turmoil</td><td>/ˈtɜːrmɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，动荡，骚动</td><td>in political turmoil<br>处于政治动荡中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country remained in political turmoil for nearly a decade.  这个国家在近十年里一直处于政治动荡之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 upheaval</td><td>/əpˈhiːvəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>剧变，动乱</td><td>social upheaval<br>社会剧变</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The war brought decades of social upheaval to the region.  战争给这一地区带来数十年的社会剧变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uproar</td><td>/ˈʌˌprɔːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，骚动</td><td>cause an uproar<br>引起轩然大波</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax plan caused an uproar among small business owners.  新税收方案在小企业主中引起轩然大波。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calm</td><td>/ˈkɑːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词、名词</td><td>平静的；使镇静</td><td>calm down quickly<br>很快平静下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a calm voice until the children settled.  她用平静的声音说话，直到孩子们安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>magnificent</b></td><td>/mæɡˈnɪfɪsnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>壮丽的</td><td>a magnificent view<br>壮丽的景色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the summit we enjoyed a magnificent view of the whole valley.  从山顶我们欣赏到整个山谷的壮丽景色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 august</td><td>/ɔːˈɡʌst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>威严的，庄重的</td><td>an august assembly<br>庄严的集会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The august assembly of scholars rose when the old president entered.  当老校长进场时，这个威严的学者集会全体起立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 epic</td><td>/ˈepɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏大的，超凡脱俗的</td><td>an epic journey<br>宏大的旅程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their epic journey across the desert took nearly four months on foot.  他们徒步穿越沙漠的宏大旅程花了近四个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glorious</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːriəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>辉煌的，壮丽的</td><td>a glorious summer sunset<br>壮丽的夏日落日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mountains looked glorious in the late afternoon light.  傍晚的光线下群山显得壮丽辉煌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grand</td><td>/ˈɡrænd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏大的，壮丽的</td><td>a grand staircase<br>宏伟的楼梯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They held the ceremony in a grand marble hall.  他们在宏伟的大理石厅里举行仪式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperial</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，令人惊叹的</td><td>imperial grandeur<br>帝王般的宏伟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hall was decorated on an imperial scale that dazzled every visitor.  大厅以帝王般宏大的规模装饰，令每位来客惊叹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imposing</td><td>/ɪmˈpoʊzɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏伟壮丽的</td><td>an imposing building<br>宏伟的建筑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An imposing stone building dominates the square at the city center.  一座气势宏伟的石砌建筑主宰着市中心的广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 massive</td><td>/ˈmæsɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，厚重的</td><td>a massive iron gate<br>厚重的铁门</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A massive wave struck the harbor wall just after midnight.  午夜刚过，一个巨浪撞上了防波堤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monumental</td><td>/ˌmɑːnjəˈmentəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>意义重大的；巨大的</td><td>a monumental achievement<br>意义重大的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Building the cathedral was a monumental task that took two centuries.  建造这座大教堂是耗时两个世纪的浩大工程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 noble</td><td>/ˈnoʊbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>高贵的，贵族的</td><td>a noble bearing<br>高贵的气派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The horse moved with a noble grace across the field.  那匹马以高贵的优雅姿态穿过田野。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 regal</td><td>/ˈriːɡəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>帝王般的，华贵威严的</td><td>a regal manner<br>帝王般的气派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She entered the hall with a regal calm that silenced everyone.  她带着帝王般的从容走进大厅，全场安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 splendid</td><td>/ˈsplendɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>壮丽的；极好的</td><td>a splendid marble hall<br>壮丽的大理石厅堂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the roof we had a splendid view of the harbor.  从屋顶望去，港口的景色极为壮丽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unimpressive</td><td>/ˌʌnɪmˈpresɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平淡无奇的，不起眼的</td><td>an unimpressive debut<br>平淡的首次登场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unimpressive debut gave no hint of the fame ahead.  他平淡的首次登场丝毫看不出日后的名声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>maladroit</b></td><td>/ˌmæləˈdrɔɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的</td><td>a maladroit attempt<br>笨拙的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His maladroit attempt at an apology only made the situation worse.  他笨拙的道歉尝试只是让情况变得更糟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 awkward</td><td>/ˈɔːkwərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的，不灵巧的；尴尬的</td><td>an awkward silence<br>尴尬的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An awkward silence followed his remark about her divorce.  他谈到她离婚之后，出现了一阵尴尬的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bumbling</td><td>/ˈbʌmbəlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨手笨脚的，办事不力的</td><td>a bumbling assistant<br>笨手笨脚的助手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bumbling waiter spilled hot soup on two guests.  那个笨手笨脚的侍者把热汤洒在两位客人身上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clumsy</td><td>/ˈklʌmzi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的，不灵巧的</td><td>a clumsy attempt<br>笨拙的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My clumsy attempt at juggling ended with three broken plates.  我笨拙地学抛球，结果打碎了三个盘子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fumbled</td><td>/ˈfʌmbəld/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>笨拙摆弄的；漏接的</td><td>fumbled the ball<br>漏接了球</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nervous speaker fumbled his notes and lost his place completely.  紧张的演讲者笨拙地翻弄笔记，完全找不到位置了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gauche</td><td>/ɡoʊʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的，缺乏社交经验的</td><td>a gauche remark<br>笨拙失礼的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His gauche remark about her salary embarrassed the whole table.  他关于她薪水的笨拙失礼的话让整桌人都很尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 graceless</td><td>/ˈɡreɪsləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的，不优雅的</td><td>a graceless heavy stride<br>笨重不雅的步态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His graceless attempt at an apology made things worse.  他笨拙的道歉尝试让事情更糟了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heavy-handed</td><td>/ˌhevi ˈhændɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的；严厉专断的</td><td>heavy-handed policing<br>粗暴的执法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government's heavy-handed response angered many peaceful protesters.  政府的强硬做法激怒了许多和平抗议者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inept</td><td>/ɪˈnept/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚笨的，荒谬的；不称职的，无能力的</td><td>politically inept<br>政治上无能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager was so inept at handling complaints that customers left in droves.  这位经理处理投诉如此无能，导致顾客大量流失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unhandy</del></td><td>/ʌnˈhændi/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不便使用的；笨手笨脚的</td><td>an unhandy tool<br>不好用的工具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This unhandy old ladder is difficult to carry upstairs.  这把不好用的旧梯子很难搬上楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adroit</td><td>/əˈdrɔɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟练的，灵巧的</td><td>adroit at negotiation<br>擅长谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She proved remarkably adroit at handling hostile questions from reporters.  她在应对记者的敌意提问时表现得非常娴熟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 ambidexterous</del></td><td>/ˌæmbɪˈdɛkstrəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>双手同样灵巧的</td><td>an ambidexterous pianist<br>双手同样灵巧的钢琴家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Being ambidexterous, he could write neatly with either hand.  由于双手都灵巧，他两只手都能写得工整。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deft</td><td>/deft/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>灵巧的，熟练的</td><td>a deft touch<br>娴熟的手法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 With a few deft strokes the artist finished the small sketch.  画家几笔灵巧的勾画便完成了那幅小素描。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dexterous</td><td>/ˈdekstrəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>动作灵活的；头脑灵活的</td><td>dexterous fingers<br>灵巧的手指</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The surgeon's dexterous hands worked steadily for more than six hours.  这位外科医生灵活的双手稳稳地工作了六个多小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 handy</td><td>/ˈhændi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>方便的；就在手边的</td><td>come in handy<br>派上用场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Keep a flashlight handy in case the power fails tonight.  把手电筒放在手边，以防今晚停电。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>malaise</b></td><td>/məˈleɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不舒服</td><td>a general sense of malaise<br>普遍的不适感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A general sense of malaise spread through the office after the layoffs.  裁员之后，一种普遍的不适感在办公室蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debility</td><td>/dəˈbɪləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>虚弱，衰弱</td><td>physical debility after illness<br>病后的身体虚弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Months of fever left him in a state of debility.  数月的发烧使他身体十分虚弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decrepitude</td><td>/dɪˈkrepɪtuːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>衰老，破败</td><td>sink into decrepitude<br>陷入破败衰颓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old theater has slid into complete decrepitude.  那家老剧院已彻底破败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disease</td><td>/dɪˈziːz/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>疾病，病症</td><td>a rare blood disease<br>罕见的血液病</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Clean drinking water prevents many diseases in the village.  干净的饮用水能预防村里许多疾病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 feebleness</td><td>/ˈfiːbəlnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>虚弱，无力</td><td>the feebleness of old age<br>年老带来的体弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His feebleness was obvious as he climbed the narrow stairs.  他爬那道窄楼梯时虚弱得很明显。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infirmity</td><td>/ɪnˈfɜːrmɪti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>体弱，疾病</td><td>the infirmities of age<br>老年的体弱多病</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Despite growing infirmity, she kept teaching until eighty.  尽管日渐体弱，她一直教到八十岁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 infirmness</del></td><td>/ɪnˈfɜːrmnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>衰弱，体弱多病</td><td>infirmness of body<br>身体的衰弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Age brought an infirmness that kept her indoors all winter.  年龄带来的体弱使她整个冬天都待在屋里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 sickliness</del></td><td>/ˈsɪklinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>多病，病弱</td><td>childhood sickliness<br>童年时的病弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sickliness as a child kept her out of school for years.  她儿时多病，好几年没能上学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unhealthiness</del></td><td>/ʌnˈhɛlθinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不健康，不卫生</td><td>the unhealthiness of the diet<br>这种饮食的不健康</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors warned about the unhealthiness of the damp old housing.  医生警告说潮湿的老房子不利健康。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>malcontent</b></td><td>/ˌmælkənˈtent/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>不满分子；不满的</td><td>a group of malcontents<br>一群不满分子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general dismissed the critics as a few malcontents.  将军把批评者说成是几个不满分子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 complainer</td><td>/kəmˈpleɪnər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>抱怨者，爱发牢骚的人</td><td>a constant complainer<br>整天抱怨的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nobody wants to sit beside a constant complainer at dinner.  没人想在饭桌上挨着一个总抱怨的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 faultfinder</del></td><td>/ˈfɔːltˌfaɪndər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>吹毛求疵的人</td><td>a constant faultfinder<br>总爱挑刺的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team leader was a constant faultfinder who praised nobody.  这位组长总爱挑刺，从不表扬任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grouch</td><td>/ˈɡraʊtʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>抱怨；牢骚满腹的人</td><td>grouch about the rules<br>抱怨规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He grouched about the new rules but followed them anyway.  他抱怨新规定，但还是照做了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rebel</td><td>/rɪˈbel/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>反抗，造反；反叛者</td><td>rebel against authority<br>反抗权威</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students rebelled against the strict new curfew last spring.  学生们去年春天反抗严苛的新宵禁令。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discontented</td><td>/ˌdɪskənˈtentɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不满足的，不满意的</td><td>discontented workers<br>不满的工人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Low pay left the staff increasingly discontented with management.  低薪使员工对管理层越来越不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discontent</td><td>/ˌdɪskənˈtent/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>不满，不满意</td><td>widespread discontent<br>普遍的不满</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rising prices caused growing discontent among the workers.  物价上涨引起工人越来越大的不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disgruntled</td><td>/dɪsˈɡrʌntld/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不满的，不高兴的</td><td>a disgruntled customer<br>心怀不满的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A disgruntled employee leaked the memo to the press.  一名心怀不满的员工把备忘录泄露给了媒体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 displeased</td><td>/dɪsˈpliːzd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不高兴的，不满的</td><td>displeased with the result<br>对结果不满</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The director was clearly displeased with the rehearsal.  导演显然对排练不满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissatisfied</td><td>/dɪˈsætəsˌfaɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不满意的</td><td>dissatisfied with the service<br>对服务不满意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many readers were dissatisfied with the book's ending.  许多读者对这本书的结局不满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ungratified</del></td><td>/ʌnˈɡrætɪfaɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未得满足的，不满意的</td><td>ungratified ambition<br>未得满足的野心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He left the meeting ungratified and quietly angry.  他离开会议时心有不满，暗自生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 contented</td><td>/kənˈtentəd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>满足的，知足的</td><td>a contented smile<br>满足的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He lived a quiet, contented life in the countryside.  他在乡下过着平静而满足的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fulfilled</td><td>/fʊlˈfɪld/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感到满足的，充实的</td><td>feel fulfilled at work<br>在工作中感到充实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She feels fulfilled teaching children in her own hometown.  在家乡教孩子让她感到充实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gratified</td><td>/ˈɡrætəˌfaɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感到满意的，欣慰的</td><td>gratified by the praise<br>因受表扬而欣慰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was gratified to hear that his advice had really helped.  听说自己的建议真的起了作用，他很欣慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pleased</td><td>/ˈpliːzd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>满意的，高兴的</td><td>pleased with the result<br>对结果感到满意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher looked pleased with our group presentation.  老师对我们的小组展示看起来很满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 satisfied</td><td>/ˈsætəsˌfaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>满意的，满足的</td><td>a satisfied customer<br>满意的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She looked satisfied with the results of the experiment.  她对实验结果看上去很满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>malicious</b></td><td>/məˈlɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶意的</td><td>a malicious rumor<br>恶意的谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A malicious rumor about the candidate spread quickly across social media.  一条关于这位候选人的恶意谣言在社交媒体上迅速传播。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bad</td><td>/ˈbæd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶意的；坏的，糟的</td><td>bad intentions<br>不良的居心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was no bad feeling between the two old rivals.  两位老对手之间并没有恶意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cruel</td><td>/ˈkruːəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>残酷的，无情的</td><td>a cruel joke<br>残忍的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was cruel to leave the dog out in the storm.  把狗留在暴风雨里太残忍了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 despiteful</del></td><td>/dɪˈspaɪtfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶意的，怀恨的</td><td>a despiteful remark<br>一句恶意的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her despiteful comments hurt him more than she realized.  她恶意的评论比她意识到的更伤他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evil</td><td>/ˈiːvəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>邪恶；邪恶的</td><td>the lesser evil<br>较小的祸害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel examines how ordinary people come to commit evil.  这部小说探讨普通人如何走向作恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 malevolent</td><td>/məˈlevələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶意的，恶毒的</td><td>a malevolent glare<br>恶毒的目光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man gave the boys a malevolent glare and slammed the gate.  老人恶毒地瞪了那些男孩一眼，砰地关上了大门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 malign</td><td>/məˈlaɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>恶意的；诽谤</td><td>malign his motives<br>诋毁他的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rival newspapers began to malign the senator without offering a shred of evidence.  竞争对手的报纸开始诽谤这位参议员，却拿不出一丝证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mean</td><td>/miːn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑贱的；吝啬的</td><td>a mean little room<br>简陋卑贱的小屋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family lived in a mean little room above a noisy printing shop.  一家人住在嘈杂印刷店楼上一间简陋的小屋里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nasty</td><td>/ˈnæsti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，肮脏的；恶劣的</td><td>a nasty smell<br>难闻的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A nasty stain spread slowly across the pale carpet.  一块难看的污渍在浅色地毯上慢慢蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spiteful</td><td>/ˈspaɪtfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶意的，怀恨的</td><td>a spiteful remark<br>一句恶毒的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sent a spiteful message to her former business partner.  她给前生意伙伴发了一条恶毒的消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vicious</td><td>/ˈvɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶恶的，恶毒的</td><td>a vicious circle<br>恶性循环</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog gave a vicious bite that required several stitches.  那只狗凶恶地咬了一口，需要缝几针。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 virulent</td><td>/ˈvɪrələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶毒的，充满敌意的；毒性强的</td><td>a virulent personal attack<br>恶毒的人身攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist launched a virulent attack on the new mayor.  这位专栏作家对新市长发起了恶毒的攻击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wicked</td><td>/ˈwɪkɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邪恶的</td><td>a wicked scheme<br>邪恶的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wicked scheme to seize the estate was uncovered by the lawyer.  律师揭穿了一个夺取遗产的邪恶计谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benevolent</td><td>/bəˈnevələnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，行善的</td><td>a benevolent employer<br>仁慈的雇主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A benevolent stranger paid for the family's train tickets.  一位仁慈的陌生人为这家人买了火车票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benign</td><td>/bɪˈnaɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的</td><td>a benign influence<br>有益无害的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in policy proved benign for most small farmers.  事实证明，政策变化对大多数小农户是无害的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 benignant</del></td><td>/bɪˈnɪɡnənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，宽厚的</td><td>a benignant smile<br>慈祥的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old abbot listened with a benignant expression on his face.  老修院长面带慈祥的神情倾听着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 charitable</td><td>/ˈtʃærətəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽容的，仁慈的</td><td>a charitable view<br>宽容的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He took a charitable view of his student's careless mistake.  他对学生的粗心错误持宽容态度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 kind</td><td>/ˈkaɪnd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>仁慈的，友好的；种类</td><td>a kind word<br>一句善意的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was kind of her to visit the sick neighbor daily.  她每天去看望生病的邻居，真是好心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 kindly</td><td>/ˈkaɪndli/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>和善的；亲切地</td><td>a kindly old man<br>一位和善的老人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A kindly neighbor carried her groceries up the steep stairs.  一位和善的邻居帮她把杂货搬上陡楼梯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>malign</b></td><td>/məˈlaɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>恶意的；诽谤</td><td>malign his motives<br>诋毁他的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rival newspapers began to malign the senator without offering a shred of evidence.  竞争对手的报纸开始诽谤这位参议员，却拿不出一丝证据。</td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her despiteful comments hurt him more than she realized.  她恶意的评论比她意识到的更伤他。</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 evil</td><td>/ˈiːvəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>邪恶；邪恶的</td><td>the lesser evil<br>较小的祸害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel examines how ordinary people come to commit evil.  这部小说探讨普通人如何走向作恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>

<tr><td>↳近 malevolent</td><td>/məˈlevələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶意的，恶毒的</td><td>a malevolent glare<br>恶毒的目光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man gave the boys a malevolent glare and slammed the gate.  老人恶毒地瞪了那些男孩一眼，砰地关上了大门。</td></tr>
</tbody>

<tr><td>↳近 malicious</td><td>/məˈlɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶意的</td><td>a malicious rumor<br>恶意的谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A malicious rumor about the candidate spread quickly across social media.  一条关于这位候选人的恶意谣言在社交媒体上迅速传播。</td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family lived in a mean little room above a noisy printing shop.  一家人住在嘈杂印刷店楼上一间简陋的小屋里。</td></tr>
</tbody>

<tr><td>↳近 nasty</td><td>/ˈnæsti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，肮脏的；恶劣的</td><td>a nasty smell<br>难闻的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A nasty stain spread slowly across the pale carpet.  一块难看的污渍在浅色地毯上慢慢蔓延。</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 spiteful</td><td>/ˈspaɪtfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶意的，怀恨的</td><td>a spiteful remark<br>一句恶毒的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sent a spiteful message to her former business partner.  她给前生意伙伴发了一条恶毒的消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vicious</td><td>/ˈvɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶恶的，恶毒的</td><td>a vicious circle<br>恶性循环</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog gave a vicious bite that required several stitches.  那只狗凶恶地咬了一口，需要缝几针。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 virulent</td><td>/ˈvɪrələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶毒的，充满敌意的；毒性强的</td><td>a virulent personal attack<br>恶毒的人身攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist launched a virulent attack on the new mayor.  这位专栏作家对新市长发起了恶毒的攻击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wicked</td><td>/ˈwɪkɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邪恶的</td><td>a wicked scheme<br>邪恶的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wicked scheme to seize the estate was uncovered by the lawyer.  律师揭穿了一个夺取遗产的邪恶计谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 asperse</del></td><td>/əˈspɜrs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诽谤，中伤</td><td>asperse his character<br>中伤他的品格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlet aspersed every officer who supported the reform.  这本小册子中伤了每一位支持改革的官员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blacken</td><td>/ˈblækən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>使变黑；诽谤，玷污</td><td>blacken his reputation<br>玷污他的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rumors quickly blackened the name of the honest official.  谣言很快玷污了这位清廉官员的名声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 calumniate</del></td><td>/kəˈlʌmnɪeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>诽谤，造谣，中伤</td><td>calumniate a rival<br>诽谤竞争对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Opponents calumniated the mayor with stories no one could prove.  反对者用无人能证实的说法诽谤市长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 defame</td><td>/dɪˈfeɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诽谤，中伤</td><td>defame a public figure<br>诽谤公众人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sued the paper for trying to defame him.  他起诉那家报纸企图诽谤他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 libel</td><td>/ˈlaɪbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>书面诽谤；（以文字）诽谤</td><td>sue for libel<br>以诽谤罪起诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The actor claimed the article libeled him and his family.  这位演员称该文诽谤了他和他的家人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smear</td><td>/ˈsmɪr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>诽谤，抹黑；涂抹</td><td>a smear campaign<br>抹黑运动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rivals tried to smear the candidate with unfounded financial rumors.  对手试图用毫无根据的财务谣言抹黑这位候选人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 traduce</del></td><td>/trəˈduːs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诽谤，中伤</td><td>traduce an opponent<br>中伤对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rival papers traduced the senator throughout the long campaign.  对立的报纸在整场竞选中中伤这位参议员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vilify</td><td>/ˈvɪlɪfaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诽谤，辱骂</td><td>vilify the whistleblower<br>诽谤举报者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The newspaper tried to vilify the whistleblower instead of examining her evidence.  报纸试图诽谤举报者，而不是审查她的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benevolent</td><td>/bəˈnevələnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，行善的</td><td>a benevolent employer<br>仁慈的雇主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A benevolent stranger paid for the family's train tickets.  一位仁慈的陌生人为这家人买了火车票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benign</td><td>/bɪˈnaɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的</td><td>a benign influence<br>有益无害的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in policy proved benign for most small farmers.  事实证明，政策变化对大多数小农户是无害的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 benignant</del></td><td>/bɪˈnɪɡnənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，宽厚的</td><td>a benignant smile<br>慈祥的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old abbot listened with a benignant expression on his face.  老修院长面带慈祥的神情倾听着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 charitable</td><td>/ˈtʃærətəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽容的，仁慈的</td><td>a charitable view<br>宽容的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He took a charitable view of his student's careless mistake.  他对学生的粗心错误持宽容态度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 kind</td><td>/ˈkaɪnd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>仁慈的，友好的；种类</td><td>a kind word<br>一句善意的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was kind of her to visit the sick neighbor daily.  她每天去看望生病的邻居，真是好心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 kindly</td><td>/ˈkaɪndli/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>和善的；亲切地</td><td>a kindly old man<br>一位和善的老人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A kindly neighbor carried her groceries up the steep stairs.  一位和善的邻居帮她把杂货搬上陡楼梯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acclaim</td><td>/əˈkleɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>称赞</td><td>win critical acclaim<br>赢得评论界赞誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won wide critical acclaim across Europe and Asia.  她的第一部小说在欧洲和亚洲赢得了广泛的评论界赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 applaud</td><td>/əˈplɔːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>称赞，赞成；鼓掌</td><td>applaud the decision<br>赞成这一决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teachers applauded the decision to reduce homework for young pupils.  老师们赞成给低年级学生减少作业的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eulogize</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>称赞；颂扬</td><td>eulogize the late leader<br>颂扬已故领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues gathered to eulogize the scientist who had trained them all.  同事们聚在一起颂扬这位培养了他们所有人的科学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extol</td><td>/ɪkˈstoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美；吹捧</td><td>extol his virtues<br>赞美他的品德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors still extol his kindness during the long winter flood.  邻居们至今仍赞美他在漫长冬季洪水期间的善举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>malinger</b></del></td><td>/məˈlɪŋɡər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>装病以逃避工作</td><td>malinger to avoid duty<br>装病逃避职责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several workers tried to malinger during the busiest week of the harvest season.  有几名工人在收获季最忙的一周里试图装病逃工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 goldbrick</del></td><td>/ˈɡoʊldbrɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>逃避工作和责任</td><td>goldbrick on the job<br>上班偷懒摸鱼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two workers were fired for goldbricking during the busiest week of the year.  两名工人因在全年最忙的一周偷懒躲活而被解雇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shirk</td><td>/ʃɜːrk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>逃避，规避</td><td>shirk responsibility<br>逃避责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one on this team is allowed to shirk hard work.  这个团队里没人可以逃避苦活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 confront</td><td>/kənˈfrʌnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>直接对抗，直面</td><td>confront the problem<br>直面问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sooner or later you must confront the problem directly.  你早晚必须直面这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 face</td><td>/ˈfeɪs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>面对，正视；脸，面</td><td>face the truth<br>正视事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sooner or later she must face the truth about the debt.  她迟早得正视这笔债务的真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meet</td><td>/miːt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合适的</td><td>a meet response<br>合适的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A public apology seemed the only meet response to such a serious blunder.  公开道歉似乎是对如此严重失误唯一合适的回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>malleable</b></td><td>/ˈmæliəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可塑的</td><td>malleable metals like gold<br>像黄金一样可塑的金属</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Gold and other malleable metals can be hammered into thin sheets.  黄金和其他易延展的金属可以被锤成薄片。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moldable</td><td>/ˈmoʊldəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可塑的，易成形的</td><td>soft moldable clay<br>柔软可塑的黏土</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm wax is moldable enough for a child to shape.  温热的蜡足够柔软，孩子也能捏出形状。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waxy</td><td>/ˈwæksi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>似蜡的，柔软易变形的</td><td>a waxy texture<br>蜡状的质地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thick petals felt waxy and cool between her fingers.  厚厚的花瓣在她指间摸起来像蜡一样凉滑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ductile</td><td>/ˈdʌktaɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有延展性的；易受影响的</td><td>ductile metal<br>有延展性的金属</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young recruits are ductile and quickly absorb the habits of the older soldiers.  新兵易受影响，很快就学到老兵的习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elastic</td><td>/ɪˈlæstɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有弹性的；灵活的</td><td>an elastic waistband<br>有弹性的腰带</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These trousers have an elastic waistband, so they fit almost anyone.  这条裤子有松紧腰带，几乎任何人都能穿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fluid</td><td>/ˈfluːɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>易变的，不固定的；流体</td><td>a fluid situation<br>变动不定的局势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The schedule remains fluid until the visas finally arrive.  在签证最终到手前，日程仍不固定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 modifiable</td><td>/ˈmɑdəˌfaɪəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可修改的，可调整的</td><td>a modifiable design<br>可修改的设计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All settings in the program are modifiable by the user.  程序中所有设置都可由用户修改。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plastic</td><td>/ˈplæstɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可塑的；易受影响的</td><td>a plastic personality<br>易受塑造的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young children have remarkably plastic minds and absorb new habits quickly.  幼儿的心智极为可塑，能很快吸收新习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pliable</td><td>/ˈplaɪəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易弯曲的，柔软的；易受影响的，温顺的</td><td>pliable young minds<br>易受影响的年轻心智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young minds are pliable and easily shaped by the adults around them.  年轻的心智易受影响，很容易被身边的成年人塑造。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pliant</td><td>/ˈplaɪənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易弯曲的；顺从的</td><td>a pliant assistant<br>顺从的助手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wanted a pliant assistant who would never question his decisions.  他想要一个顺从的助手，永远不会质疑他的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supple</td><td>/ˈsʌpl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易弯曲的，柔软的</td><td>supple leather<br>柔软的皮革</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gloves were made of supple leather that fit her hands perfectly.  这双手套用柔软的皮革制成，和她的手完全贴合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 variable</td><td>/ˈveriəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>可变的，多变的</td><td>variable costs<br>可变成本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Winds in the strait are variable and often dangerous.  海峡里的风多变且常常危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adamant</td><td>/ˈædəmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，不可动摇的</td><td>adamant about the rules<br>对规则毫不通融</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach was adamant that no player would skip practice.  教练坚决要求没有球员可以缺席训练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intractable</td><td>/ˌɪnˈtræktəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难对付的，棘手的</td><td>an intractable problem<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poverty remains an intractable problem in many rural regions.  贫困在许多农村地区仍是棘手的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 recalcitrant</td><td>/rɪˈkælsɪtrənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽抗的，不顺从的</td><td>a recalcitrant employee<br>不听劝的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A recalcitrant employee refused every instruction his new supervisor gave him.  一名顽抗的员工拒绝了新主管给他的每一条指示。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 refractory</td><td>/rɪˈfræktəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>倔强的，不顺从的</td><td>a refractory pupil<br>不顺从的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One refractory pupil disrupted the lesson for the entire class.  一个不顺从的学生扰乱了全班的课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ungovernable</td><td>/ʌnˈɡʌvərnəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以控制的，无法管束的</td><td>an ungovernable temper<br>无法控制的脾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd became ungovernable once the gates were opened.  大门一开，人群就变得无法控制。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unmanageable</td><td>/ənˈmænɪdʒəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以管理的，难控制的</td><td>unmanageable debt<br>难以控制的债务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The class grew unmanageable after the substitute arrived.  代课老师来后，这个班变得难以管理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unruly</td><td>/ʌnˈruːli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难驾驭的，不守规矩的</td><td>an unruly crowd<br>不守规矩的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two teachers struggled to calm the unruly class.  两位老师费力地安抚那个不守规矩的班级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>malodorous</b></td><td>/ˌmælˈoʊdərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶臭的</td><td>malodorous waste<br>恶臭的废弃物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors traced the malodorous discharge to a nearby factory.  检查人员追查到那股恶臭排放来自附近一家工厂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fetid</td><td>/ˈfetɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶臭的</td><td>fetid air<br>恶臭的空气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fetid smell rose from the blocked drain behind the house.  屋后堵塞的下水道散出恶臭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foul</td><td>/ˈfaʊl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>玷污（名誉）；弄脏</td><td>foul one's good name<br>玷污自己的好名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scandal fouled the good name he had spent decades building.  这桩丑闻玷污了他数十年积累的好名声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 frowsy</del></td><td>/ˈfraʊzi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的</td><td>a frowsy old coat<br>邋遢的旧外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked frowsy after sleeping two nights on the train.  在火车上睡了两夜后，他显得很邋遢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 funky</td><td>/ˈfʌŋki/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有难闻气味的，发臭的</td><td>a funky smell<br>难闻的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A funky smell came from the lunch bag he had forgotten.  他忘掉的午餐袋里发出难闻的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fusty</td><td>/ˈfʌsti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有霉味的，霉臭的；过时的</td><td>a fusty old room<br>一间霉味的旧房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They opened every window to air out the fusty old room.  他们打开每扇窗给这间霉味的旧房间通风。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 musty</td><td>/ˈmʌsti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>发霉的，霉味的；陈腐的</td><td>a musty smell<br>一股霉味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cellar was dark, damp, and full of musty old books.  地窖阴暗潮湿，堆满带霉味的旧书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 noisome</td><td>/ˈnɔɪsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶臭的；令人极厌恶的</td><td>a noisome odor<br>一股恶臭气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers refused to enter the noisome tunnel without masks.  工人们不戴口罩不肯进入那条恶臭的隧道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rank</td><td>/ˈræŋk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶臭的；过于茂盛的</td><td>a rank smell of sweat<br>刺鼻的汗臭味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cellar had a rank smell of mold and damp earth.  地窖里有一股霉味和湿土的恶臭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 reeky</del></td><td>/ˈriːki/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>臭气熏人的</td><td>a reeky old blanket<br>臭气熏人的旧毯子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He threw the reeky fishing clothes straight into the wash.  他把臭气熏人的钓鱼衣直接扔进洗衣机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smelly</td><td>/ˈsmeli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难闻的，有臭味的</td><td>smelly socks<br>有臭味的袜子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bin in the corner was getting distinctly smelly.  角落里的垃圾桶明显开始发臭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stinking</td><td>/ˈstɪŋkɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>发臭的，恶臭的</td><td>a stinking pile of rubbish<br>一堆发臭的垃圾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They cleared a stinking heap of garbage from the alley.  他们清走了巷子里一堆发臭的垃圾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stinky</td><td>/ˈstɪŋki/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>臭的，恶臭的</td><td>a stinky cheese<br>一种臭味的奶酪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That stinky cheese tastes far better than it smells.  那种臭奶酪的味道远比气味好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ambrosial</td><td>/æmˈbroʊziəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>特别美味的；芬芳的</td><td>an ambrosial dessert<br>美味无比的甜点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soup had an ambrosial aroma that filled the whole house.  这汤散发的美妙香气弥漫了整座房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aromatic</td><td>/ˌerəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>芳香的</td><td>aromatic herbs<br>芳香的草药</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was full of aromatic spices from the market.  厨房里满是从市场买来的芳香香料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fragrant</td><td>/ˈfreɪɡrənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>芳香的，香的</td><td>fragrant flowers<br>芳香的花朵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was fragrant with fresh bread and cinnamon.  厨房里弥漫着新鲜面包和肉桂的香气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 perfumed</td><td>/pərˈfjuːmd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有香味的，洒了香水的</td><td>perfumed soap<br>香皂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wrote the letter on thin perfumed paper.  她用薄薄的香纸写了那封信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 redolent</td><td>/ˈredələnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>芳香的；令人想起…的</td><td>redolent of spices<br>散发香料气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old room was redolent of pipe smoke and leather.  那间旧屋弥漫着烟斗烟味和皮革气息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 savory</td><td>/ˈseɪvəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的，可口的</td><td>a savory dish<br>一道可口的菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She served a savory pie made with mushrooms and herbs.  她端上一道用蘑菇和香草做的美味派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scented</td><td>/ˈsentɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有香味的，加香的</td><td>scented candles<br>香味蜡烛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wrote all her letters on pale scented paper.  她所有的信都写在淡色的香味纸上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sweet</td><td>/ˈswiːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>甜的；温和的，亲切的</td><td>in a sweet tone<br>以温和的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a sweet voice that calmed the frightened child.  她用温柔的声音说话，安抚了害怕的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mandatory</b></td><td>/ˈmændətɔːri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强制的</td><td>a mandatory requirement<br>强制性要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Safety training is mandatory for all new employees here.  这里所有新员工都必须接受安全培训。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compulsory</td><td>/kəmˈpʌlsəri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强制的，必修的</td><td>compulsory education<br>义务教育</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wearing a helmet is compulsory on every construction site.  在每个建筑工地戴安全帽都是强制的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forced</td><td>/ˈfɔːrst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>被迫的，强迫的</td><td>a forced confession<br>被迫的供词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court threw out the forced confession as unreliable.  法庭以不可靠为由驳回了这份被迫的供词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperative</td><td>/ɪmˈperətɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>命令的，强制性的；迫切的</td><td>an imperative need<br>迫切的需要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is imperative that we repair the dam before the rains arrive.  在雨季来临前修好大坝是迫切必需的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incumbent</td><td>/ɪnˈkʌmbənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>义不容辞的，必须的</td><td>incumbent on the manager<br>经理义不容辞的事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is incumbent on every driver to check the brakes regularly.  定期检查刹车是每位司机义不容辞的责任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 involuntary</td><td>/ɪnˈvɑːlənteri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非自愿的；无意识的</td><td>an involuntary reaction<br>不自觉的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Blinking is an involuntary response to sudden light.  眨眼是对突然强光的不自觉反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 necessary</td><td>/ˈnesəˌseri/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>必需的，必要的；必需品</td><td>a necessary expense<br>一项必要的开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Clean water is necessary for even the simplest household.  即便最简朴的家庭也需要干净的水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obligatory</td><td>/əˈblɪɡətɔːri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强制性的</td><td>obligatory military service<br>强制的兵役</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In that country obligatory military service lasts for eighteen months.  在那个国家，强制的兵役要服十八个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peremptory</td><td>/pəˈremptəri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不容置疑的；专横傲慢的</td><td>a peremptory order<br>不容置疑的命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His peremptory tone left no room for questions or discussion.  他傲慢的语气不给任何提问或讨论的余地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 required</td><td>/rɪˈkwaɪərd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>必需的，规定的</td><td>required reading<br>必读材料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A signed form is required before the trip can begin.  出行前必须提交一份签好的表格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 elective</td><td>/ɪˈlektɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>可自由选择的；选举的</td><td>an elective course<br>选修课程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most of the surgery performed here is elective, not urgent.  这里做的多数手术是可选择的，而非紧急的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 optional</td><td>/ˈɑːpʃənəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可选择的，非强制的</td><td>an optional extra course<br>一门选修的额外课程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Attendance at the evening seminar is entirely optional.  参加晚间研讨会完全是自愿的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 voluntary</td><td>/ˈvɑːlənteri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自愿的，志愿的</td><td>voluntary participation<br>自愿参与</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Attendance at the evening seminar is entirely voluntary.  晚间研讨会完全自愿参加。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mangle</b></td><td>/ˈmæŋɡl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>弄砸</td><td>mangle the song<br>把歌唱得一塌糊涂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The amateur band completely mangled the song at last night's concert.  那支业余乐队在昨晚的音乐会上把这首歌彻底演砸了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blow</td><td>/ˈbloʊ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>挥霍，乱花钱</td><td>blow one's savings<br>挥霍掉积蓄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He will blow his entire bonus on a week in Tokyo.  他会把整笔奖金挥霍在东京的一周上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bumble</td><td>/ˈbʌmbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>含糊不清地说，杂乱无章地说</td><td>bumble through a speech<br>含糊地讲完演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He bumbled through his answer and confused the whole audience.  他含糊其辞地答完，把全场听众都弄糊涂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bungle</td><td>/ˈbʌŋɡl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>办糟，失败</td><td>bungle the job<br>把工作办糟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crew bungled the repair and flooded the entire basement.  工人把维修办糟了，整个地下室都被水淹了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 butcher</td><td>/ˈbʊtʃər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>弄糟，搞得一塌糊涂；屠宰</td><td>butcher the translation<br>把翻译弄得一塌糊涂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The band completely butchered my favorite old song.  这支乐队把我最爱的老歌演得一塌糊涂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fumble</td><td>/ˈfʌmbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>笨拙地做</td><td>fumble for the keys<br>笨拙地摸找钥匙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He fumbled for the keys in his dark coat pocket.  他在昏暗的外套口袋里笨拙地摸找钥匙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mar</td><td>/mɑːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>破坏，削弱</td><td>mar the celebration<br>破坏庆典气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A careless remark at dinner managed to mar an otherwise perfect evening.  餐桌上一句轻率的话毁掉了本该完美的一晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mess</td><td>/ˈmes/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>混乱，脏乱；弄乱</td><td>make a mess of it<br>把它弄得一团糟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was a terrible mess when the children finished baking.  孩子们烤完点心后厨房一片狼藉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ruin</td><td>/ˈruːɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>毁坏，毁灭；废墟</td><td>ruin the harvest<br>毁掉收成</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Weeks of drought ruined the harvest and emptied the village granary.  数周干旱毁掉收成，村里粮仓也空了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foul up</td><td>/faʊl ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>弄糟，搞乱</td><td>foul up the schedule<br>把日程搞乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless email fouled up the whole delivery plan.  一封粗心的邮件把整个送货计划搞乱了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 screw up</td><td>/skru ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>办糟，搞砸</td><td>screw up an interview<br>把面试搞砸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He screwed up his first interview but got the job anyway.  他把第一次面试搞砸了，但最后还是拿到了工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mangy</b></td><td>/ˈmeɪndʒi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>肮脏破旧的，邋遢的</td><td>a mangy old coat<br>破旧邋遢的旧外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wore a mangy overcoat that looked as though it had survived two wars.  他穿着一件破旧邋遢的大衣，看上去像是经历过两场战争。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 base</td><td>/ˈbeɪs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑劣的，低下的</td><td>base motives<br>卑劣的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He acted from base motives of greed and envy.  他出于贪婪和嫉妒的卑劣动机行事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contemptible</td><td>/kənˈtemptəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可鄙的，卑劣的</td><td>contemptible behavior<br>卑劣的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheating a blind beggar is a contemptible thing to do.  欺骗盲丐是可鄙的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debased</td><td>/dəˈbeɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>堕落的，贬损的</td><td>debased taste<br>低劣的品味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He complained about the debased state of public debate.  他抱怨公共辩论的堕落状态。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 despicable</td><td>/dɪˈspɪkəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人鄙视的</td><td>a despicable act<br>令人鄙视的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stealing from a charity is a despicable way to make money.  从慈善机构偷钱是一种令人鄙视的赚钱方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detestable</td><td>/dɪˈtɛstəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，令人厌恶的</td><td>a detestable habit<br>令人厌恶的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He found the general's casual cruelty absolutely detestable.  他觉得那位将军随意的残暴极其可憎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 execrable</td><td>/ˈeksɪkrəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>糟糕的，恶劣的</td><td>execrable table manners<br>极糟糕的餐桌礼仪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel served execrable coffee at breakfast every morning.  酒店每天早餐提供的咖啡都糟糕透了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mean</td><td>/miːn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑贱的；吝啬的</td><td>a mean little room<br>简陋卑贱的小屋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family lived in a mean little room above a noisy printing shop.  一家人住在嘈杂印刷店楼上一间简陋的小屋里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sordid</td><td>/ˈsɔːrdɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>肮脏的，不干净的；卑鄙的</td><td>a sordid little room<br>肮脏的小房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They lived for years in a sordid little room without heat.  他们在没有暖气的肮脏小屋里住了多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squalid</td><td>/ˈskwɑːlɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>污秽的，肮脏的</td><td>squalid living conditions<br>肮脏的居住条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The refugees endured squalid living conditions in the overcrowded camp.  难民在过度拥挤的营地里忍受肮脏的居住条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lofty</td><td>/ˈlɔːfti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>崇高的；自大的</td><td>a lofty tone<br>自大的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dismissed our idea with a lofty wave of her hand.  她自大地一挥手就否定了我们的想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 noble</td><td>/ˈnoʊbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>高贵的，贵族的</td><td>a noble bearing<br>高贵的气派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The horse moved with a noble grace across the field.  那匹马以高贵的优雅姿态穿过田野。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 upright</td><td>/ˈʌpraɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>正直的，诚实的；竖直的</td><td>an upright citizen<br>一位正直的公民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors described him as an upright and reliable man.  邻居们说他是个正直可靠的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 venerable</td><td>/ˈvenərəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古老而受尊敬的</td><td>a venerable institution<br>历史悠久的机构</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The venerable library has served scholars for over four centuries.  这座古老而受人尊敬的图书馆已为学者服务了四百多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 virtuous</td><td>/ˈvɜːrtʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>品德高尚的，正直的</td><td>a virtuous life<br>高尚的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He led a quiet, virtuous life and helped his neighbors freely.  他过着平静高尚的生活，乐于帮助邻里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mania</b></td><td>/ˈmeɪniə/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>热衷，狂热</td><td>a mania for collecting<br>对收藏的狂热</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her mania for collecting antique clocks filled every room of the old house.  她对收集古董钟表的狂热塞满了老房子的每个房间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ardor</td><td>/ˈɑːrdər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>狂热</td><td>revolutionary ardor<br>革命热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young recruits sang with revolutionary ardor all the way to the front.  年轻的新兵一路满怀革命热情地高唱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 craze</td><td>/ˈkreɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>一时的狂热，风尚</td><td>the latest fitness craze<br>最新的健身热潮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Skateboarding became a craze at our school that spring.  那年春天滑板在我们学校成了热潮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
