<tr><td>↳近 enthusiasm</td><td>/ɪnˈθuːziæzəm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>热情，热忱</td><td>greet the plan with enthusiasm<br>热情地迎接该计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her enthusiasm for the project convinced the whole committee.  她对这个项目的热情说服了整个委员会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fervor</td><td>/ˈfɜːrvər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>热情，热烈</td><td>patriotic fervor<br>爱国热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd sang the anthem with surprising fervor.  人群以惊人的热情唱起国歌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obsession</td><td>/əbˈseʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>痴迷，念念不忘</td><td>an obsession with detail<br>对细节的痴迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His obsession with old maps filled the entire attic.  他对旧地图的痴迷塞满了整个阁楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 passion</td><td>/ˈpæʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>热情，激情</td><td>a passion for music<br>对音乐的热爱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He talks about old trains with genuine passion.  他谈起老火车时充满真挚的热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preoccupation</td><td>/priˌɑːkjuˈpeɪʃn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>全神贯注；念念不忘之事</td><td>a preoccupation with money<br>一心只想着钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her preoccupation with the exam kept her awake all night.  她一心想着考试，整夜没睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 prepossession</del></td><td>/ˌpriːpəˈzeʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>先入之见；专注</td><td>an early prepossession<br>早先形成的成见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His prepossession with rank colored every judgment he made.  他对地位的执念影响了他所有的判断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 zeal</td><td>/ziːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>热心，热诚</td><td>religious zeal<br>宗教热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took up the campaign with the zeal of a true believer.  她以真正信徒的热诚投入了这场运动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apathy</td><td>/ˈæpəθi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>缺乏兴趣，不关心</td><td>public apathy toward voting<br>公众对投票的冷漠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Widespread apathy among young voters worried the campaign organizers.  年轻选民中普遍的冷漠让竞选组织者担忧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indifference</td><td>/ɪnˈdɪfərəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>漠不关心，冷淡</td><td>treat with indifference<br>以冷淡的态度对待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd greeted the announcement with total indifference.  人群对这个通告完全漠不关心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nonchalance</td><td>/ˌnɑːnʃəˈlɑːns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冷漠，漠不关心</td><td>studied nonchalance<br>刻意的淡然</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She accepted the important award with surprising nonchalance.  她以出人意料的淡然接受了这个重要奖项。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 torpor</td><td>/ˈtɔːrpər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>缺乏兴趣；（肉体或精神上的）迟钝，懒散</td><td>sink into torpor<br>陷入迟钝麻木</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heat sank the whole village into a heavy afternoon torpor.  暑热让整个村子陷入沉沉的午后麻木。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>manifest</b></td><td>/ˈmænɪfest/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>显然的，明显易懂的；显现，显露</td><td>manifest injustice<br>明显的不公</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His discomfort at the question was manifest to everyone seated around the table.  他对这个问题的不适让围坐桌边的每个人都看得明明白白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apparent</td><td>/əˈperənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的；表面上的</td><td>for no apparent reason<br>没有明显原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It soon became apparent that the machine had never worked.  很快就明显看出这台机器从未运转过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clear</td><td>/ˈklɪr/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，确凿的；清楚的</td><td>a clear majority<br>明显的多数</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party won by a clear majority of votes.  该党以明显多数票获胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinct</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>清晰的，明显的</td><td>a distinct impression<br>清晰的印象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a distinct difference between the two dialects.  这两种方言之间有明显差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evident</td><td>/ˈevɪdənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显然的</td><td>clearly evident<br>显而易见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her talent for music was evident from a very early age.  她的音乐才华从很小就显而易见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lucid</td><td>/ˈluːsɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达清晰的，易懂的</td><td>a lucid explanation<br>清晰的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor offered a lucid summary of a difficult argument.  教授对一个艰深论证做了清晰的总结。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obvious</td><td>/ˈɑːbviəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显而易见的</td><td>an obvious mistake<br>明显的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The answer seemed obvious once she explained the method.  她解释了方法后，答案似乎显而易见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 palpable</td><td>/ˈpælpəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，易觉察的</td><td>a palpable sense of relief<br>明显的宽慰感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A palpable tension filled the room as the verdict was read aloud.  宣读判决时，房间里弥漫着明显的紧张气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 patent</td><td>/ˈpeɪtnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>显而易见的，明显的；专利</td><td>a patent lie<br>显而易见的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The excuse was a patent lie and everyone knew it.  这个借口是显而易见的谎言，人人都知道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 perspicuous</del></td><td>/pərˈspɪkjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表述清晰的，明白易懂的</td><td>a perspicuous style<br>明白易懂的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His writing is perspicuous even when the topic is technical.  即使话题很专业，他的文章也清晰易懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plain</td><td>/ˈpleɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>完全的，十足的；朴素的，清楚的</td><td>plain stupid<br>简直愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ignoring the warning was just plain stupid.  忽视警告纯粹是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transparent</td><td>/trænsˈpærənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有歧义的，清晰易懂的</td><td>a transparent explanation<br>清晰易懂的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a transparent explanation that even beginners understood immediately.  他给出清晰易懂的解释，连初学者都立刻明白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bespeak</td><td>/bɪˈspiːk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>表明，显示；预示</td><td>bespeak great care<br>显示出极大用心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The neat handwriting bespeaks a patience few students still possess.  整洁的字迹表明少有学生仍具备的耐心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 betray</td><td>/bɪˈtreɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>无意中显露，表明；背叛</td><td>betray his nervousness<br>显露他的紧张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His trembling hands betrayed the fear his calm voice concealed.  他颤抖的双手泄露了平静语调所掩藏的恐惧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demonstrate</td><td>/ˈdemənstreɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（通过证据）证明，表明</td><td>demonstrate the theory clearly<br>清楚地证明该理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The experiment demonstrated the theory clearly to every skeptical observer.  实验向每位持疑的观察者清楚证明了该理论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 display</td><td>/dɪsˈpleɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>展示，陈列；显露</td><td>display the paintings<br>展出这些画作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum displays its rarest coins in a locked glass case.  博物馆把最珍稀的钱币陈列在上锁的玻璃柜里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evince</td><td>/ɪˈvɪns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>表明</td><td>evince little interest<br>表现出兴趣不大</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His flat answers evinced very little interest in the job.  他平淡的回答表明他对这份工作兴趣不大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expose</td><td>/ɪkˈspoʊz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露；使暴露</td><td>expose the fraud<br>揭露欺诈行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two journalists exposed the fraud after months of quiet investigation.  两名记者经过数月暗中调查揭露了这起欺诈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reveal</td><td>/rɪˈviːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭示，透露</td><td>reveal the details<br>透露细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company refused to reveal the details of the settlement.  公司拒绝透露和解的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cryptic</td><td>/ˈkrɪptɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>秘密的；难以理解的</td><td>a cryptic remark<br>费解的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His cryptic note gave us no hint about where he had gone.  他那张费解的便条没有透露他去了哪里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enigmatic</td><td>/ˌenɪɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>神秘的，难以理解的</td><td>an enigmatic smile<br>神秘的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her enigmatic answer left the reporters guessing for several days.  她神秘的回答让记者们猜了好几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indistinct</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊不清的，含糊的</td><td>an indistinct outline<br>模糊的轮廓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph shows an indistinct figure by the door.  照片上门边有个模糊的人影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mysterious</td><td>/mɪˈstɪriəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>神秘的，难以理解的</td><td>a mysterious stranger<br>神秘的陌生人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A mysterious light appeared over the lake around midnight.  午夜时分湖面上出现了一道神秘的光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obfuscated</td><td>/ˈɑbfəskeɪtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>混淆不清的，弄糊涂的</td><td>obfuscated legal language<br>晦涩的法律语言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report was so obfuscated that nobody could find the real numbers.  这份报告写得如此含混，没人能找到真实数据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obscure</td><td>/əbˈskjʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>含义模糊的；平凡的，不知名的</td><td>an obscure poet<br>不知名的诗人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an obscure poet until a critic rediscovered his work decades later.  他一直是个不知名的诗人，直到几十年后一位批评家重新发现他的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unclear</td><td>/ʌnˈklɪr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不清楚的，不明确的</td><td>remain unclear<br>仍不明确</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is still unclear who made the final decision.  谁做的最终决定仍不清楚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conceal</td><td>/kənˈsiːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>隐藏，隐瞒</td><td>conceal the truth<br>隐瞒真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness tried to conceal the truth from the police.  证人试图向警方隐瞒真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hide</td><td>/ˈhaɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>隐藏，隐瞒</td><td>hide the evidence<br>藏匿证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She hid the letters in a drawer beneath her sweaters.  她把信藏在毛衣下面的抽屉里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>manipulate</b></td><td>/məˈnɪpjuleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>巧妙处理；暗中操控</td><td>manipulate public opinion<br>操控公众舆论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The campaign tried to manipulate public opinion by releasing carefully edited video clips.  竞选团队试图通过发布精心剪辑的视频片段来操控公众舆论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 machinate</del></td><td>/ˈmækəˌneɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>阴谋策划，图谋</td><td>machinate against a rival<br>图谋对付对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ministers machinated for months to remove the young king.  大臣们策划了数月要废掉年轻的国王。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maneuver</td><td>/məˈnuːvər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>巧妙地操纵</td><td>maneuver the car into place<br>把车巧妙地挪到位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She patiently maneuvered the car into a very narrow parking space.  她耐心地把车挪进一个非常狭窄的停车位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mannered</b></td><td>/ˈmænərd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不自然的，做作的</td><td>a mannered writing style<br>做作的写作风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her mannered prose impressed critics at first but soon began to feel tiresome.  她做作的文风起初让评论家赞叹，但很快就令人厌倦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affected</td><td>/əˈfektɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>装出来的，做作的</td><td>an affected accent<br>装出来的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke with an affected accent that fooled nobody present.  她用装出来的口音说话，没骗过在场任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 artificial</td><td>/ˌɑːrtəˈfɪʃəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>人造的；虚假的</td><td>artificial flowers<br>人造花</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lobby was decorated with artificial flowers that never wilted.  大厅用永不枯萎的人造花装饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assumed</td><td>/əˈsuːmd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假装的，假冒的</td><td>under an assumed name<br>用假名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He traveled under an assumed name for several years.  他用假名旅行了好几年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 factitious</td><td>/fækˈtɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>人为的，虚假的</td><td>factitious enthusiasm<br>装出来的热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The applause sounded factitious and died away very quickly.  掌声听起来很假，很快就停了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fake</td><td>/ˈfeɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词、动词</td><td>假的，伪造的；伪造品</td><td>a fake diploma<br>假文凭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallery unknowingly displayed a fake for almost ten years.  这家画廊不知情地展出了一件赝品近十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 feigned</td><td>/ˈfeɪnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假装的，伪装的</td><td>feigned surprise<br>假装的惊讶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He listened with feigned interest while checking his phone.  他一边看手机，一边假装感兴趣地听着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretended</td><td>/priˈtendɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假装的，虚伪的</td><td>pretended concern<br>假装的关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her pretended surprise did not convince anyone in the room.  她假装的惊讶没能说服屋里任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pseudo</td><td>/ˈsuːdoʊ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假的，冒充的</td><td>pseudo science<br>伪科学</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The book dresses up old superstitions as pseudo science.  这本书把旧迷信包装成伪科学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sham</td><td>/ʃæm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>掩饰，假装；虚假的</td><td>a sham marriage<br>假婚姻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The couple admitted their marriage was a sham arranged for the visa.  这对夫妻承认他们的婚姻是为签证安排的假婚姻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spurious</td><td>/ˈspjʊriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假的，伪造的</td><td>spurious claims<br>虚假的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The website spread spurious claims about the new vaccine.  该网站传播关于新疫苗的虚假说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 natural</td><td>/ˈnætʃərəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自然的，正常的</td><td>a natural reaction<br>自然的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is natural to feel nervous before a big exam.  大考前紧张是很自然的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>manumit</b></del></td><td>/ˌmænjuˈmɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解放（奴隶）</td><td>manumit his slaves<br>解放他的奴隶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dying planter chose to manumit every slave who had worked his fields.  这位垂死的种植园主决定解放所有为他耕作过的奴隶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 free</td><td>/ˈfriː/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>释放，解放；自由的</td><td>free the hostages<br>释放人质</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Negotiators finally persuaded the gunmen to free the hostages.  谈判者最终说服枪手释放了人质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 emancipate</td><td>/ɪˈmænsɪpeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解放，解除束缚</td><td>emancipate the slaves<br>解放奴隶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new law emancipated the slaves working on the northern plantations.  新法律解放了在北方种植园劳作的奴隶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enfranchise</td><td>/ɪnˈfræntʃaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>给予…权利（例如选举权）；解放</td><td>enfranchise women<br>赋予妇女选举权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The amendment enfranchised millions of citizens for the first time.  该修正案首次赋予数百万公民选举权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 liberate</td><td>/ˈlɪbəreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解放，释放</td><td>liberate the city<br>解放这座城市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Allied troops liberated the city in the spring of that year.  盟军在那年春天解放了这座城市。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loose</td><td>/ˈluːs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>释放，放开；松的</td><td>loose the dogs<br>放开猎狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The keeper loosed the hawk and watched it climb into the sky.  饲养员放开那只鹰，看它飞向天空。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 release</td><td>/riˈliːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>释放，解除束缚；发布</td><td>release the prisoners<br>释放囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court ordered officials to release him from custody at once.  法庭命令官员立即将他从拘留中释放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unbind</td><td>/ʌnˈbaɪnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开，松开，释放</td><td>unbind the prisoner<br>解开囚犯的绑缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She knelt down to unbind the wounded man's hands.  她跪下来解开那名伤者被绑的双手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unchain</td><td>/ʌnˈtʃeɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开锁链，释放</td><td>unchain the dog<br>把狗解开链子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The keeper unchained the old elephant and let it wander freely.  饲养员解开老象的链子，让它自由走动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unshackle</td><td>/ʌnˈʃækəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开束缚，释放</td><td>unshackle the economy<br>解除对经济的束缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reforms unshackled small businesses from needless paperwork.  改革让小企业摆脱了无谓的文书负担。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 detain</td><td>/dɪˈteɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拘留，扣押；留住</td><td>detain a suspect<br>拘留嫌疑人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police detained the two men for questioning about the missing car.  警方拘留了这两名男子，就失踪汽车一事问话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>manuscript</b></td><td>/ˈmænjəskrɪpt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>手稿</td><td>an ancient manuscript<br>一份古代手稿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library keeps a medieval manuscript under tightly controlled lighting.  图书馆将一份中世纪手稿保存在严格控制的灯光下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 calligraphy</td><td>/kəˈlɪɡrəfi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>（优美的）书法</td><td>practice Chinese calligraphy<br>练习中国书法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He practices Chinese calligraphy for an hour every evening.  他每天晚上练一小时中国书法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 penmanship</td><td>/ˈpɛnmənʃɪp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>书法，书写技艺</td><td>neat penmanship<br>工整的书写</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His penmanship was so neat that teachers displayed his notebooks.  他的书写工整得让老师把他的本子展示出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 script</td><td>/skrɪpt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>手写体，字体；剧本</td><td>in elegant script<br>以优雅的字体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The invitation was written in flowing italic script.  邀请函以流畅的斜体字写成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 print</td><td>/ˈprɪnt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>印刷；印刷字体</td><td>a book in small print<br>小号字印刷的书</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The publisher printed only five hundred copies of the poem.  出版社只印了五百册这首诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 type</td><td>/ˈtaɪp/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>打字；类型，铅字</td><td>type a clean copy<br>打出一份清稿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She typed the whole manuscript in three long nights.  她用三个漫长的夜晚把整份手稿打了出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 typewriting</td><td>/ˈtaɪˌpraɪtɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>打字；打字稿</td><td>a page of typewriting<br>一页打字稿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old file contained neat typewriting from the 1950s.  这份旧档案里是五十年代的整齐打字稿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mar</b></td><td>/mɑːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>破坏，削弱</td><td>mar the celebration<br>破坏庆典气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A careless remark at dinner managed to mar an otherwise perfect evening.  餐桌上一句轻率的话毁掉了本该完美的一晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blemish</td><td>/ˈblemɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>缺点，污点；损害，玷污</td><td>a blemish on his record<br>他履历上的污点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One late payment left a small blemish on his credit record.  一次逾期付款在他的信用记录上留下了小小的污点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compromise</td><td>/ˈkɑːmprəmaɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>妥协，折中；危害</td><td>reach a compromise<br>达成妥协</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After long debate the committee reached a workable compromise.  经过长时间辩论，委员会达成了可行的妥协。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cripple</td><td>/ˈkrɪpəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使严重受损，削弱</td><td>cripple the economy<br>严重削弱经济</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strike crippled the city's transport system for a week.  罢工使该市交通系统瘫痪了一周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 damage</td><td>/ˈdæmədʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>损害，破坏；损失</td><td>damage the crops<br>损害庄稼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Late frost damaged the crops across the entire river valley.  晚霜损害了整个河谷的庄稼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deface</td><td>/dɪˈfeɪs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>损毁外观，涂污</td><td>deface a monument<br>损毁纪念碑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone defaced the statue with spray paint during the night.  有人夜里用喷漆涂污了那座雕像。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disfigure</td><td>/dɪsˈfɪɡjər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>损毁外形，使难看</td><td>disfigure the facade<br>损毁建筑正面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap repairs disfigured the elegant facade of the old theater.  廉价的修补损毁了老剧院优雅的正面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harm</td><td>/ˈhɑːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>伤害，损害</td><td>harm the environment<br>损害环境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pesticide runoff harms the fish living in the nearby stream.  农药径流危害了附近溪流中的鱼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hurt</td><td>/ˈhɜːrt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>伤害，使疼痛</td><td>hurt his feelings<br>伤害他的感情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Your careless comment clearly hurt her feelings at the dinner.  你随口的评论显然在晚餐上伤了她的感情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impair</td><td>/ɪmˈper/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>损害，削弱</td><td>impair his judgment<br>削弱他的判断力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lack of sleep seriously impairs judgment and slows your reactions.  睡眠不足严重损害判断力并减慢反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 injure</td><td>/ˈɪndʒər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使受伤，伤害</td><td>injure his knee<br>伤到他的膝盖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He injured his knee while running downhill on wet stones.  他在湿滑石头上跑下坡时伤了膝盖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spoil</td><td>/ˈspɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>损坏，破坏；溺爱</td><td>spoil the surprise<br>破坏惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy rain spoiled the picnic we had planned for weeks.  大雨破坏了我们筹划数周的野餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vitiate</td><td>/ˈvɪʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>削弱，损害；使无效</td><td>vitiate an agreement<br>使协议失效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single false statement can vitiate the entire contract.  一句虚假陈述就能使整份合同失效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 doctor</td><td>/ˈdɑːktər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>篡改，窜改；医生</td><td>doctor the accounts<br>篡改账目</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was accused of doctoring the financial records last year.  他被指控篡改了去年的财务记录。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fix</td><td>/ˈfɪks/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>修理，解决；固定</td><td>fix the leaking pipe<br>修好漏水的管子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A neighbor fixed the leaking pipe before the floor was ruined.  邻居在地板毁坏前修好了漏水的管子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mend</td><td>/ˈmend/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>纠正，改正；修补</td><td>mend his ways<br>改正他的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He promised to mend his ways after the second stern warning.  第二次严厉警告后他承诺改正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 patch</td><td>/ˈpætʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>斑块，小片；补丁</td><td>a damp patch<br>一块潮湿的斑迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a damp patch on the ceiling above the door.  门上方的天花板上有一块潮湿的斑迹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 renovate</td><td>/ˈrenəveɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>修复，维修</td><td>renovate the old house<br>翻修老房子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We must renovate the old house completely before moving in.  搬进去之前我们必须彻底翻修这栋老房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repair</td><td>/rɪˈper/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>修理，修补</td><td>repair the roof<br>修补屋顶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers repaired the roof just before the rainy season began.  工人们在雨季开始前修好了屋顶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>malfeasance</b></td><td>/mælˈfiːzəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>非法行为</td><td>official malfeasance<br>公职人员的违法行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The auditor uncovered years of malfeasance in the city office.  审计员查出市政办公室多年的违法行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misconduct</td><td>/ˌmɪsˈkɑːndʌkt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不当行为，渎职</td><td>professional misconduct<br>职业上的不当行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two officers were dismissed for serious misconduct last month.  上个月两名警官因严重不当行为被解职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misbehavior</td><td>/ˌmɪsbɪˈheɪvjər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不良行为，品行不端</td><td>repeated misbehavior in class<br>课堂上屡次不守规矩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school called his parents about his constant misbehavior.  学校因他不断的不良行为叫来了家长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 misdoing</del></td><td>/ˌmɪsˈduɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶行，不法行为</td><td>confess one's misdoings<br>承认自己的过错</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was never punished for his earlier misdoings.  他早年的不法行为从未受到惩罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wrongdoing</td><td>/ˈrɔːŋduɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>不法行为，坏事</td><td>admit any wrongdoing<br>承认有任何不当行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company denied any wrongdoing in the contract.  公司否认在这份合同中有任何不法行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>martinet</b></td><td>/ˌmɑːrtnˈet/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>纪律严明之人</td><td>a strict martinet<br>极严厉的执纪之人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their new sergeant was a martinet who inspected every button before morning drill.  他们的新中士是个执纪严苛的人，晨操前连纽扣都要检查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disciplinarian</td><td>/ˌdɪsɪplɪˈneriən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>严格执行纪律的人</td><td>a strict disciplinarian<br>严厉守纪律的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their grandfather was a strict disciplinarian about bedtime.  他们的祖父在睡觉时间上极其严格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 purist</td><td>/ˈpjʊrɪst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>纯粹主义者，拘守传统的人</td><td>a language purist<br>语言纯正主义者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Purists complain that the new translation is far too casual.  纯粹主义者抱怨新译本太随意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stickler</td><td>/ˈstɪklər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>坚持细节的人，一丝不苟的人</td><td>a stickler for rules<br>死守规则的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our new manager is a stickler for rules about punctuality.  我们的新经理在守时规则上一丝不苟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reprobate</td><td>/ˈreprəbeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>堕落者，道德败坏的人</td><td>an old reprobate<br>一个老无赖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel's hero is a charming reprobate with no money.  小说的主角是个迷人却身无分文的堕落者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>marvel</b></td><td>/ˈmɑːrvl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>惊叹，惊奇；令人惊奇的事物</td><td>marvel at the view<br>对景色惊叹不已</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Visitors still marvel at the vaulted ceiling of the cathedral after eight centuries.  八百年后，参观者仍对这座大教堂的拱顶惊叹不已。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flash</td><td>/ˈflæʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>闪光；闪耀，闪现</td><td>a flash of lightning<br>一道闪电</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A light flashed briefly in the window across the street.  街对面的窗子里闪了一下光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 miracle</td><td>/ˈmɪrəkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>奇迹，不可思议之事</td><td>a medical miracle<br>医学上的奇迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a miracle that everyone walked away from the crash.  所有人都从车祸中走了出来，真是奇迹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phenomenon</td><td>/fəˈnɑːməˌnɑːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>现象；非凡的人或事</td><td>a natural phenomenon<br>一种自然现象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scientists still cannot fully explain this strange phenomenon.  科学家仍无法完全解释这个奇怪现象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prodigy</td><td>/ˈprɑːdədʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>天才，奇才；奇事</td><td>a child prodigy<br>神童</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young violinist was hailed as a prodigy at eight.  这位年轻小提琴手八岁时就被誉为神童。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 splendor</td><td>/ˈsplendər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>壮丽，华美</td><td>in all its splendor<br>尽显其壮丽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The restored palace now stands in all its former splendor.  修复后的宫殿如今重现昔日的壮丽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gape</td><td>/ˈɡeɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>目瞪口呆地看，张口凝视</td><td>gape in astonishment<br>惊讶地张口凝视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tourists gaped at the collapsed tower for hours.  游客们盯着倒塌的塔看了好几个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gaze</td><td>/ˈɡeɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>凝视，注视</td><td>gaze at the stars<br>凝望星空</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They gazed at the mountains until the sun had finally set.  他们凝望群山，直到太阳终于落下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 goggle</td><td>/ˈɡɑːɡl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>凝视</td><td>goggle at the screen<br>凝视屏幕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tourists goggled at the enormous statue for several silent minutes.  游客们默默凝视那尊巨大的雕像好几分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wonder</td><td>/ˈwʌndər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>奇观，奇迹；惊奇</td><td>one of the world's wonders<br>世界奇观之一</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children stared at the waterfall in silent wonder.  孩子们默默惊叹地望着瀑布。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mash</b></td><td>/mæʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>捣碎</td><td>mash the boiled potatoes<br>把煮好的土豆捣碎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mash the boiled potatoes thoroughly before mixing in the warm milk.  在拌入温牛奶之前把煮好的土豆彻底捣碎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crush</td><td>/krʌʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>压碎，碾碎</td><td>crush garlic with a knife<br>用刀把蒜压碎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He crushed the dry leaves into powder between his palms.  他用手掌把干叶子搓成了粉末。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grind</td><td>/ˈɡraɪnd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>磨碎，磨光；苦干</td><td>grind the lens smooth<br>把镜片磨光滑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers ground the marble surface until it shone.  工人把大理石表面磨到发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smash</td><td>/ˈsmæʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>轰动的成功；打碎</td><td>a box-office smash<br>票房大热之作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The low-budget comedy became a box-office smash overnight.  这部低成本喜剧一夜之间成为票房大热。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squeeze</td><td>/ˈskwiːz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>挤压，压缩；紧握</td><td>squeeze a lemon<br>挤柠檬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She squeezed the tube until the last bit of paste came out.  她挤压软管直到最后一点膏体出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 agglomerate</td><td>/əˈɡlɑːməreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>结块，聚成一团</td><td>agglomerate into lumps<br>聚结成块</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wet powder agglomerated into hard grey lumps.  湿粉结成了灰色的硬块。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>masquerade</b></td><td>/ˌmæskəˈreɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>面具，伪装</td><td>a masquerade of concern<br>假装关心的伪装</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His politeness was only a masquerade concealing deep resentment toward his colleagues.  他的客气只是掩盖对同事深深怨恨的伪装。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 facade</td><td>/fəˈsɑːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>假象，伪装；建筑正面</td><td>a facade of calm<br>平静的假象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Behind a facade of confidence he was deeply worried.  在自信的表象背后，他忧心不已。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 guise</td><td>/ˈɡaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>外观，伪装的样子</td><td>under the guise of<br>以…为幌子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He entered the palace under the guise of a merchant.  他以商人为幌子进入了宫殿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mask</td><td>/ˈmæsk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>掩盖，掩饰；面具</td><td>mask his anger<br>掩饰他的怒气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud music masked the sound of the approaching helicopter.  响亮的音乐掩盖了直升机靠近的声音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretense</td><td>/ˈpriːtens/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>假装，伪装；借口</td><td>under false pretenses<br>以虚假的名义</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He obtained the money under false pretenses and was arrested months later.  他以虚假的名义骗取钱财，几个月后被逮捕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 semblance</td><td>/ˈsembləns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>表象，外表；些许</td><td>a semblance of order<br>表面上的秩序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the storm the town regained some semblance of normal life.  风暴过后小镇恢复了几分正常生活的样子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 show</td><td>/ˈʃoʊ/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>显示，展现；展览</td><td>show your feelings<br>表露感情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rarely shows his feelings, even to close friends.  他很少表露感情，连对好友也如此。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 veil</td><td>/ˈveɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>遮掩，掩饰；面纱</td><td>veil his true motives<br>掩饰真实动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Polite words could not veil his true motives for long.  客气话没能长久掩饰他的真实动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 act</td><td>/ˈækt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>装样子，表演；行动</td><td>act for the crowd<br>为观众做样子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was only acting hurt to win the crowd's sympathy.  他只是装作受伤，以博取观众同情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disguise</td><td>/dɪsˈɡaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>伪装，掩饰；伪装</td><td>disguise his intentions<br>掩饰他的意图</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He disguised his disappointment behind a polite and steady smile.  他用礼貌平静的微笑掩饰了失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pose</td><td>/ˈpoʊz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>假装，冒充；姿态</td><td>pose as an expert<br>冒充专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He posed as a doctor in order to get into the ward.  他冒充医生以便进入病房。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretend</td><td>/prɪˈtend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>假装，装作</td><td>pretend to be asleep<br>装睡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children pretended not to hear their mother calling.  孩子们假装没听见母亲呼唤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 betray</td><td>/bɪˈtreɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>无意中显露，表明；背叛</td><td>betray his nervousness<br>显露他的紧张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His trembling hands betrayed the fear his calm voice concealed.  他颤抖的双手泄露了平静语调所掩藏的恐惧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disclose</td><td>/dɪsˈkloʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露，公开，透露</td><td>disclose the details<br>公开细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company refused to disclose the details of the settlement.  公司拒绝公开和解协议的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reveal</td><td>/rɪˈviːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭示，透露</td><td>reveal the details<br>透露细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company refused to reveal the details of the settlement.  公司拒绝透露和解的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unmask</td><td>/ʌnˈmæsk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露，揭穿真面目</td><td>unmask the impostor<br>揭穿那个冒充者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The journalist unmasked the company's secret polluting practices.  记者揭露了这家公司秘密排污的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mastery</b></td><td>/ˈmæstəri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>技艺超群，精通</td><td>mastery of the violin<br>对小提琴的精通</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her mastery of the violin was obvious from the very first bar she played.  她对小提琴的精通从她演奏的第一个小节就显露无疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deftness</td><td>/ˈdɛftnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>灵巧，熟练</td><td>the deftness of her fingers<br>她手指的灵巧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He shuffled the cards with surprising deftness.  他洗牌的手法灵巧得令人惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dexterity</td><td>/dekˈsterəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>灵巧，敏捷</td><td>manual dexterity<br>手的灵巧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Surgery demands great manual dexterity and very steady nerves.  外科手术需要极高的手部灵巧和极稳的神经。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 finesse</td><td>/fɪˈnes/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>娴熟技巧；（巧妙地）躲避</td><td>handle it with finesse<br>娴熟巧妙地处理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She handled the awkward question with real finesse and everyone relaxed.  她娴熟巧妙地处理了那个尴尬的问题，大家都放松了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prowess</td><td>/ˈpraʊəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>高超技艺；英勇</td><td>athletic prowess<br>过人的运动本领</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His athletic prowess earned him a scholarship at a famous university.  他出色的运动才能为他赢得了一所名校的奖学金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 virtuosity</td><td>/ˌvɜːrtʃuˈɑːsəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精湛的技艺</td><td>technical virtuosity<br>精湛的技巧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her virtuosity at the piano silenced the whole hall.  她精湛的钢琴技艺让全场安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 amateurishness</del></td><td>/ˈæmətərɪʃnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>业余水平，不专业</td><td>the amateurishness of the design<br>设计上的不专业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics mocked the amateurishness of the film's editing.  评论者嘲笑这部片子剪辑得很业余。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>matriculate</b></td><td>/məˈtrɪkjuleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>录取</td><td>matriculate at the university<br>被大学录取入学</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only students with excellent scores matriculate at the university's competitive engineering program each fall.  每年秋天只有成绩优异的学生才能被这所大学竞争激烈的工程专业录取。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 admit</td><td>/ədˈmɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>承认；准许进入</td><td>admit the mistake<br>承认错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She finally admitted that she had made a serious mistake.  她终于承认自己犯了一个严重的错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enroll</td><td>/ɪnˈroʊl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>注册，报名入学</td><td>enroll in a course<br>报名参加课程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She enrolled in evening classes to finish her degree.  她报名上夜校以完成学位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inscribe</td><td>/ɪnˈskraɪb/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>登记，注册；铭刻</td><td>inscribe his name<br>把他的名字登记上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His name was inscribed in the college register that autumn.  那年秋天他的名字被登记进学院名册。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recruit</td><td>/rəˈkruːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>新成员，新兵；招募</td><td>a raw recruit<br>毫无经验的新兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every new recruit spends six weeks in basic training.  每名新兵都要接受六周基础训练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 register</td><td>/ˈredʒɪstər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>登记，记录</td><td>register a complaint<br>登记一项投诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You must register your bicycle with the city office.  你必须在市政办公室登记自行车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commence</td><td>/kəˈmens/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>开始，着手（正式）</td><td>commence legal proceedings<br>开始法律诉讼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Construction will commence next month if funding is approved.  若资金获批，工程将于下月开始。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 graduate</td><td>/ˈɡrædʒueɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>毕业；毕业生</td><td>graduate from college<br>大学毕业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She graduated from law school with the highest honors.  她以最高荣誉从法学院毕业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>maudlin</b></td><td>/ˈmɔːdlɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于感伤的</td><td>maudlin sentimentality<br>过度的感伤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After two drinks he grew maudlin about his lost youth.  喝了两杯后他为逝去的青春伤感起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lachrymose</td><td>/ˈlækrɪmoʊs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>催人泪下的，悲伤的</td><td>a lachrymose farewell<br>催人泪下的告别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's lachrymose ending left half the theater reaching for tissues.  这部电影催人泪下的结尾让半个影院的人去找纸巾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mawkish</td><td>/ˈmɔːkɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过度伤感的</td><td>a mawkish film<br>滥情的电影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's mawkish ending made several people in the audience laugh out loud.  这部电影滥情的结局让观众里好几个人笑出声来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mushy</td><td>/ˈmʌʃi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多愁善感的，肉麻的；软糊的</td><td>a mushy love letter<br>肉麻的情书</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wrote her a mushy letter that he later regretted.  他给她写了一封后来觉得肉麻的信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overemotional</td><td>/ˌoʊvərɪˈmoʊʃənəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于感情化的</td><td>an overemotional reaction<br>过于情绪化的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She apologized for her overemotional speech at the farewell.  她为告别会上过于情绪化的讲话道了歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sentimental</td><td>/ˌsentəˈmentəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多情的，感情用事的</td><td>sentimental value<br>情感价值</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old watch has great sentimental value for my mother.  这块旧表对我母亲有很大的情感价值。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tearful</td><td>/ˈtɪrfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>含泪的，悲伤的</td><td>a tearful reunion<br>含泪的重逢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They said a tearful goodbye at the station gate.  他们在车站门口含泪告别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 blithe</td><td>/blaɪð/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愉快高兴的；无忧无虑的；漫不经心的</td><td>a blithe disregard<br>漫不经心的无视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His blithe disregard for the rules annoyed the older staff.  他对规则漫不经心的无视让资深员工不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cheerful</td><td>/ˈtʃɪrfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快乐的，愉快的</td><td>a cheerful greeting<br>愉快的问候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed cheerful even after the long delay.  即使长时间延误后他仍保持愉快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 jocund</del></td><td>/ˈdʒɑːkənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢快的，高兴的</td><td>a jocund gathering<br>欢快的聚会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The jocund crowd sang and danced until the last lantern went out.  欢快的人群唱歌跳舞，直到最后一盏灯笼熄灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 jovial</td><td>/ˈdʒoʊviəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愉快的</td><td>a jovial host<br>愉快的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our jovial host kept the guests laughing throughout the long winter evening.  我们愉快的主人让客人们在漫长的冬夜里笑声不断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 jubilant</td><td>/ˈdʒuːbɪlənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喜悦的</td><td>a jubilant crowd<br>欢欣的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nominee gave a short speech before the jubilant crowd.  被提名人在欢欣鼓舞的人群前发表了简短讲话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>maven</b></td><td>/ˈmeɪvn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专家</td><td>a marketing maven<br>营销专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magazine hired a marketing maven to explain why the campaign had failed.  杂志请来一位营销专家解释这次宣传为何失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ace</td><td>/ˈeɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>高手，能人</td><td>an ace at chess<br>国际象棋高手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is an ace with numbers and finished the audit alone.  他是数字高手，独自完成了审计。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adept</td><td>/əˈdept/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>内行的，熟练的；行家</td><td>adept at negotiation<br>擅长谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is remarkably adept at calming angry customers.  她极擅长安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 connoisseur</td><td>/ˌkɑːnəˈsɜːr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>鉴赏家（尤指艺术领域）；专家</td><td>a connoisseur of fine wine<br>美酒鉴赏家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a connoisseur of fine wine and rare books.  他是美酒和珍本书的鉴赏家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expert</td><td>/ˈekspərt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>专家；熟练的，专业的</td><td>a leading expert<br>顶尖专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They called in an expert to examine the cracked foundation.  他们请来专家检查开裂的地基。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maestro</td><td>/ˈmaɪstroʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大师（尤指音乐指挥家）</td><td>the aging maestro<br>年迈的大师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The maestro raised his baton and the hall fell silent.  大师举起指挥棒，全场随之安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 master</td><td>/ˈmæstər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>大师，名家；主人</td><td>a master of disguise<br>伪装大师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a master of the craft could carve such fine details.  只有行家高手才能雕出这般精细的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 professional</td><td>/prəˈfeʃənəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>专业的；专业人士</td><td>a professional musician<br>专业的音乐家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a professional could repair such a delicate mechanism.  只有专业人士才能修好如此精密的机构。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proficient</td><td>/prəˈfɪʃnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟练的，精通的</td><td>proficient in Spanish<br>精通西班牙语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After two years abroad she became proficient in Spanish and Portuguese.  在国外待了两年后，她精通了西班牙语和葡萄牙语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 virtuoso</td><td>/ˌvɜːrtʃuˈoʊsoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>技艺大师，演奏名家；技艺精湛的</td><td>a violin virtuoso<br>小提琴演奏大师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The violin virtuoso played the difficult passage without a single mistake.  那位小提琴大师毫无差错地演奏了这段难曲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amateur</td><td>/ˈæməˌtɜːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>业余爱好者；外行的</td><td>a talented amateur<br>有天赋的业余者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comet was first spotted by an amateur astronomer.  这颗彗星最先由一位业余天文学家发现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dabbler</td><td>/ˈdæblər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>浅尝辄止者，业余涉猎者</td><td>a dabbler in poetry<br>诗歌上的业余涉猎者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained a cheerful dabbler in half a dozen crafts.  他一直是好几门手艺的快乐业余涉猎者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dilettante</td><td>/ˌdɪləˈtænti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>浅尝辄止的业余爱好者；不专业的</td><td>a mere dilettante<br>只是业余涉猎者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a mere dilettante in astronomy, he owned a telescope but read little.  作为天文学上的业余涉猎者，他有望远镜却几乎不读书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>maverick</b></td><td>/ˈmævərɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>特立独行之人</td><td>a political maverick<br>政坛的独行者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a political maverick, she voted against her own party on almost every bill.  作为政坛的独行者，她几乎在每项法案上都投票反对本党。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bohemian</td><td>/boʊˈhiːmiən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>特立独行之人</td><td>a bohemian lifestyle<br>放浪不羁的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young painter led a bohemian lifestyle in a crowded attic studio.  这位年轻画家在拥挤的阁楼画室里过着放浪不羁的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deviant</td><td>/ˈdiːviənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>离常规的人；异常的</td><td>labeled a deviant<br>被视为异类</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Society once treated any deviant thinker as a genuine danger.  社会曾把任何异类思想者视为真正的危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heretic</td><td>/ˈherətɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>异端者，异教徒</td><td>burned as a heretic<br>作为异端被烧死</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In his own time the scientist was treated as a heretic.  在他那个时代，这位科学家被当作异端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 iconoclast</td><td>/aɪˈkɑːnəklæst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>特立独行的人</td><td>a fearless iconoclast<br>无畏的反传统者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a lifelong iconoclast, she questioned every rule the institute cherished.  作为一生特立独行的人，她质疑该研究所珍视的每条规则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nonconformist</td><td>/ˌnɑːnkənˈfɔːrmɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>不墨守成规的人</td><td>a lifelong nonconformist<br>终身的叛逆者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a nonconformist who questioned every rule at school.  他是个不循规蹈矩的人，质疑学校的每条规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dissentient</del></td><td>/dɪˈsɛnʃənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>持异议的；异议者</td><td>a dissentient voice<br>一个异议的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only one dissentient member voted against the proposal.  只有一名持异议的成员投票反对提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissenting</td><td>/dɪˈsentɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>持异议的，不同意的</td><td>a dissenting opinion<br>异议意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two judges wrote dissenting opinions on the final ruling.  两位法官就终审判决写了异议意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissident</td><td>/ˈdɪsədənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>持不同意见者；异议的</td><td>a political dissident<br>政治异见者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dissident writer published his essays abroad.  这位异见作家在国外发表了他的文章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heterodox</td><td>/ˈhetərədɑːks/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非正统的，异端的</td><td>heterodox opinions<br>非正统的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her heterodox economic theories were ignored for decades before gaining respect.  她非正统的经济理论被忽视数十年后才获得尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 iconoclastic</td><td>/ˌaɪkənəˈklæstɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>打破传统的，反偶像的</td><td>an iconoclastic thinker<br>打破传统的思想家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His iconoclastic views upset the older professors.  他反传统的观点让年长的教授们不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unorthodox</td><td>/ʌnˈɔːrθədɑːks/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非正统的，异端的</td><td>an unorthodox approach<br>非正统的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach used unorthodox drills that somehow worked very well.  教练用了非正统的训练，效果却出奇地好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 conformer</del></td><td>/kənˈfɔrmər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>顺从者，随大流者</td><td>a quiet conformer<br>安静的顺从者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The office rewarded the conformer and punished the original thinker.  办公室奖励顺从者，惩罚有独立想法的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conformist</td><td>/kənˈfɔːrmɪst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>墨守成规者，随大流者</td><td>a cautious conformist<br>谨慎的随大流者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In that office every conformist was rewarded and every rebel ignored.  在那个办公室，随大流者受奖，反叛者被无视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 conventionalist</del></td><td>/kənˈvɛnʃənəlɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>守旧者，因循传统的人</td><td>a cautious conventionalist<br>谨慎的守旧者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a conventionalist, he distrusted every new method.  作为一个守旧者，他不信任任何新方法。</td></tr>
</tbody>

<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's mawkish ending made several people in the audience laugh out loud.  这部电影滥情的结局让观众里好几个人笑出声来。</td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's lachrymose ending left half the theater reaching for tissues.  这部电影催人泪下的结尾让半个影院的人去找纸巾。</td></tr>
</tbody>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overemotional</td><td>/ˌoʊvərɪˈmoʊʃənəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于感情化的</td><td>an overemotional reaction<br>过于情绪化的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She apologized for her overemotional speech at the farewell.  她为告别会上过于情绪化的讲话道了歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sentimental</td><td>/ˌsentəˈmentəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多情的，感情用事的</td><td>sentimental value<br>情感价值</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old watch has great sentimental value for my mother.  这块旧表对我母亲有很大的情感价值。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tearful</td><td>/ˈtɪrfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>含泪的，悲伤的</td><td>a tearful reunion<br>含泪的重逢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They said a tearful goodbye at the station gate.  他们在车站门口含泪告别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 blithe</td><td>/blaɪð/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愉快高兴的；无忧无虑的；漫不经心的</td><td>a blithe disregard<br>漫不经心的无视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His blithe disregard for the rules annoyed the older staff.  他对规则漫不经心的无视让资深员工不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cheerful</td><td>/ˈtʃɪrfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快乐的，愉快的</td><td>a cheerful greeting<br>愉快的问候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed cheerful even after the long delay.  即使长时间延误后他仍保持愉快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 jocund</del></td><td>/ˈdʒɑːkənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢快的，高兴的</td><td>a jocund gathering<br>欢快的聚会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The jocund crowd sang and danced until the last lantern went out.  欢快的人群唱歌跳舞，直到最后一盏灯笼熄灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 jovial</td><td>/ˈdʒoʊviəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愉快的</td><td>a jovial host<br>愉快的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our jovial host kept the guests laughing throughout the long winter evening.  我们愉快的主人让客人们在漫长的冬夜里笑声不断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 jubilant</td><td>/ˈdʒuːbɪlənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喜悦的</td><td>a jubilant crowd<br>欢欣的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nominee gave a short speech before the jubilant crowd.  被提名人在欢欣鼓舞的人群前发表了简短讲话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>meager</b></td><td>/ˈmiːɡər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫乏的</td><td>a meager income<br>微薄的收入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They raised four children on a meager income from a small roadside shop.  他们靠路边小店微薄的收入养育了四个孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 exiguous</del></td><td>/ɪɡˈzɪɡjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极少的，微薄的</td><td>an exiguous budget<br>少得可怜的预算</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They survived a whole year on exiguous rations of rice.  他们靠少得可怜的米粮过了整整一年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 niggardly</td><td>/ˈnɪɡərdli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的，少得可怜的</td><td>a niggardly raise<br>少得可怜的加薪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered a niggardly raise after record profits.  在创纪录盈利后公司给了少得可怜的加薪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 poor</td><td>/pʊr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的，贫乏的；贫穷的</td><td>poor rocky soil<br>贫瘠的石质土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The harvest was poor after a whole summer without rain.  整个夏天没下雨，收成很差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scanty</td><td>/ˈskænti/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏的，稀少的</td><td>scanty evidence<br>证据不足</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Evidence for the theory remains scanty.  支持该理论的证据仍然很少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scarce</td><td>/ˈskers/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀缺的，不足的</td><td>in scarce supply<br>供应短缺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fresh water became scarce during the drought.  旱灾期间淡水变得稀缺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 skimpy</td><td>/ˈskɪmpi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不足的，少得可怜的</td><td>a skimpy portion of meat<br>少得可怜的一份肉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report gave only skimpy evidence for its bold claims.  报告只给出很少的证据来支持大胆的结论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slender</td><td>/ˈslendər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>苗条的，细长的</td><td>a slender waist<br>纤细的腰身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She held the flower by its slender stem.  她捏着那朵花细长的茎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slim</td><td>/ˈslɪm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微小的，微薄的；苗条的</td><td>a slim chance of success<br>极小的成功机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We have only a slim chance of catching the last train.  我们赶上末班车的机会很小。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sparing</td><td>/ˈsperɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>节俭的，节约的</td><td>sparing use of salt<br>节省地用盐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was sparing with salt because of his doctor's strict orders.  遵医嘱，他用盐十分节省。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sparse</td><td>/spɑːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀疏的，稀少的</td><td>sparse vegetation<br>稀疏的植被</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vegetation grows sparse as the trail climbs above the tree line.  小径爬上林线以上后，植被变得稀疏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stingy</td><td>/ˈstɪndʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>小气的，吝啬的</td><td>stingy with money<br>在钱上很吝啬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is stingy with money but surprisingly generous with his time.  他在钱上吝啬，在时间上却出奇大方。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abundant</td><td>/əˈbʌndənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，丰富的</td><td>abundant evidence<br>大量的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water was abundant after the spring rains.  春雨过后水源充足。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ample</td><td>/ˈæmpəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充足的，富裕的</td><td>ample opportunity<br>充足的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is ample room for everyone in the van.  车里有足够的空间容纳所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bountiful</td><td>/ˈbaʊntɪfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，充裕的</td><td>a bountiful harvest<br>丰盛的收成</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The valley gave them a bountiful crop of apples.  这个山谷给了他们丰盛的苹果收成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 copious</td><td>/ˈkoʊpiəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，丰富的</td><td>take copious notes<br>做大量笔记</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took copious notes during every lecture and reread them each night.  她每堂课都记大量笔记，并在每晚重读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 generous</td><td>/ˈdʒenərəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，大量的；慷慨的</td><td>a generous helping<br>满满的一份</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She poured a generous glass of wine for each guest.  她给每位客人倒了满满一杯酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 liberal</td><td>/ˈlɪbərəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>思想前卫的；慷慨的，大方的</td><td>liberal social views<br>前卫的社会观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His liberal social views often surprised the older members of the club.  他前卫的社会观念常常让俱乐部里年长的成员感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 plenteous</del></td><td>/ˈplɛntiəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，充足的</td><td>a plenteous supply of water<br>充足的水源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That year brought plenteous rain and unusually heavy crops.  那年雨水充足，庄稼格外茂盛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plentiful</td><td>/ˈplentəfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，大量的</td><td>a plentiful harvest<br>丰收</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rain came early, so the harvest was unusually plentiful.  雨来得早，所以收成异常丰富。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mean</b></td><td>/miːn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑贱的；吝啬的</td><td>a mean little room<br>简陋卑贱的小屋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family lived in a mean little room above a noisy printing shop.  一家人住在嘈杂印刷店楼上一间简陋的小屋里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 base</td><td>/ˈbeɪs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑劣的，低下的</td><td>base motives<br>卑劣的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He acted from base motives of greed and envy.  他出于贪婪和嫉妒的卑劣动机行事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contemptible</td><td>/kənˈtemptəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可鄙的，卑劣的</td><td>contemptible behavior<br>卑劣的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheating a blind beggar is a contemptible thing to do.  欺骗盲丐是可鄙的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 despicable</td><td>/dɪˈspɪkəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人鄙视的</td><td>a despicable act<br>令人鄙视的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stealing from a charity is a despicable way to make money.  从慈善机构偷钱是一种令人鄙视的赚钱方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detestable</td><td>/dɪˈtɛstəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，令人厌恶的</td><td>a detestable habit<br>令人厌恶的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He found the general's casual cruelty absolutely detestable.  他觉得那位将军随意的残暴极其可憎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dirty</td><td>/ˈdɜːrti/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>肮脏的；卑鄙的</td><td>dirty windows<br>脏污的窗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children came home with dirty hands and happy faces.  孩子们回家时手很脏，脸上却很开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dishonorable</td><td>/dɪˈsɑːnərəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不光彩的，可耻的</td><td>a dishonorable discharge<br>不光彩的除役</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Betraying an old friend for money is plainly dishonorable.  为钱背叛老友显然可耻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 execrable</td><td>/ˈeksɪkrəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>糟糕的，恶劣的</td><td>execrable table manners<br>极糟糕的餐桌礼仪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel served execrable coffee at breakfast every morning.  酒店每天早餐提供的咖啡都糟糕透了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ignominious</td><td>/ˌɪɡnəˈmɪniəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可耻的，不光彩的</td><td>an ignominious defeat<br>可耻的失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The season ended in ignominious defeat at home.  这个赛季以主场可耻的失利告终。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sordid</td><td>/ˈsɔːrdɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>肮脏的，不干净的；卑鄙的</td><td>a sordid little room<br>肮脏的小房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They lived for years in a sordid little room without heat.  他们在没有暖气的肮脏小屋里住了多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 closefisted</del></td><td>/ˌkloʊsˈfɪstɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的，一毛不拔的</td><td>a closefisted landlord<br>吝啬的房东</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their closefisted landlord refused to fix the heater.  他们吝啬的房东拒绝修暖气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 mingy</del></td><td>/ˈmɪndʒi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的；少得可怜的</td><td>a mingy tip<br>少得可怜的小费</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He left a mingy tip after a very long meal.  吃了很久之后他只留下少得可怜的小费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 miserly</td><td>/ˈmaɪzərli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的</td><td>a miserly tip<br>吝啬的小费</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He left a miserly tip after complaining about the service all evening.  他整晚抱怨服务，最后只留下吝啬的小费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 niggardly</td><td>/ˈnɪɡərdli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的，少得可怜的</td><td>a niggardly raise<br>少得可怜的加薪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered a niggardly raise after record profits.  在创纪录盈利后公司给了少得可怜的加薪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 parsimonious</td><td>/ˌpɑːrsəˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的，过分节俭的</td><td>parsimonious with praise<br>极少给出赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The parsimonious landlord refused to replace the broken heater.  吝啬的房东拒绝更换坏了的暖气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 penurious</del></td><td>/pəˈnʊriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的；吝啬的</td><td>penurious circumstances<br>贫困的境况</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent a penurious youth in a crowded tenement.  他在拥挤的廉租房里度过了贫困的青年时期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 tightfisted</del></td><td>/ˌtaɪtˈfɪstɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的</td><td>a tightfisted employer<br>吝啬的雇主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their tightfisted employer counted every paper clip.  他们吝啬的雇主连回形针都要数。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ungenerous</td><td>/ʌnˈdʒɛnərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的，不宽厚的</td><td>an ungenerous offer<br>不厚道的出价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It seemed ungenerous to refuse her such a small favor.  拒绝她这么小的请求显得很不厚道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lofty</td><td>/ˈlɔːfti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>崇高的；自大的</td><td>a lofty tone<br>自大的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dismissed our idea with a lofty wave of her hand.  她自大地一挥手就否定了我们的想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 noble</td><td>/ˈnoʊbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>高贵的，贵族的</td><td>a noble bearing<br>高贵的气派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The horse moved with a noble grace across the field.  那匹马以高贵的优雅姿态穿过田野。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 venerable</td><td>/ˈvenərəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古老而受尊敬的</td><td>a venerable institution<br>历史悠久的机构</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The venerable library has served scholars for over four centuries.  这座古老而受人尊敬的图书馆已为学者服务了四百多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 virtuous</td><td>/ˈvɜːrtʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>品德高尚的，正直的</td><td>a virtuous life<br>高尚的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He led a quiet, virtuous life and helped his neighbors freely.  他过着平静高尚的生活，乐于帮助邻里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 generous</td><td>/ˈdʒenərəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，大量的；慷慨的</td><td>a generous helping<br>满满的一份</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She poured a generous glass of wine for each guest.  她给每位客人倒了满满一杯酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 liberal</td><td>/ˈlɪbərəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>思想前卫的；慷慨的，大方的</td><td>liberal social views<br>前卫的社会观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His liberal social views often surprised the older members of the club.  他前卫的社会观念常常让俱乐部里年长的成员感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 munificent</td><td>/mjuːˈnɪfɪsnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>慷慨的</td><td>a munificent donation<br>一笔慷慨的捐赠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The university received a munificent donation from a former engineering student.  这所大学收到了一位工程系校友的慷慨捐赠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>meander</b></td><td>/miˈændər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>闲逛，漫步</td><td>meander through the park<br>在公园里闲逛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We meandered through the park all afternoon with no particular destination in mind.  我们整个下午在公园里闲逛，心里并没有特定的目的地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amble</td><td>/ˈæmbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>漫步，闲逛</td><td>amble along the beach<br>沿海滩漫步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We ambled along the river until the sun went down.  我们沿着河边漫步，直到日落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cruise</td><td>/ˈkruːz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>缓慢巡行，漫游</td><td>cruise along the coast<br>沿着海岸缓行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We cruised slowly down the river all afternoon.  我们整个下午沿河缓慢漫游。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drift</td><td>/ˈdrɪft/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>漂流，漫无目的地移动</td><td>drift from town to town<br>从一个城镇漂到另一个</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He drifted through Europe for two years without any plans.  他毫无计划地在欧洲漂了两年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 float</td><td>/ˈfloʊt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>漂浮，飘荡</td><td>float downstream slowly<br>缓缓顺流漂下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Leaves floated lazily past us on the warm current.  叶子随暖流懒懒地漂过我们身边。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ramble</td><td>/ˈræmbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>漫谈，东拉西扯；漫步</td><td>ramble on about<br>喋喋不休地扯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rambled on about his childhood for a full hour.  他絮絮叨叨讲了整整一小时童年往事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 roam</td><td>/ˈroʊm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>漫游，闲逛</td><td>roam the old streets<br>在老街上闲逛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They roamed the narrow streets until the shops closed.  他们在窄街上闲逛到商店关门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 saunter</td><td>/ˈsɔːntər/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>闲逛，漫步</td><td>saunter down the street<br>沿街闲逛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sauntered into the office half an hour late.  他迟到半小时，悠然地走进办公室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stroll</td><td>/ˈstroʊl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>散步，闲逛</td><td>take a stroll<br>去散散步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They strolled through the old market after dinner.  晚饭后他们在老市场里闲逛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wander</td><td>/ˈwɑːndər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>漫游，闲逛；走神</td><td>wander around aimlessly<br>四处漫无目的闲逛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child wandered off while his parents were shopping.  孩子在父母购物时走开乱逛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>measly</b></td><td>/ˈmiːzli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>少得可怜的，微不足道的</td><td>a measly ten dollars<br>少得可怜的十美元</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered a measly ten dollars for a painting worth several hundred.  他为一幅值几百美元的画只出了少得可怜的十美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsequential</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːnsɪˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不重要的，微不足道的</td><td>an inconsequential remark<br>无关紧要的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The difference between the two prices is quite inconsequential.  两个价格之间的差别微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsiderable</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的</td><td>an inconsiderable sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk is small but by no means inconsiderable.  风险虽小，却绝非微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insignificant</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关紧要的，微不足道的</td><td>a seemingly insignificant change<br>看似微小的改动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An insignificant error early on ruined the entire calculation.  早期一个微不足道的错误毁了整个计算。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minute</td><td>/maɪˈnjuːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微不足道的；细致的</td><td>a minute quantity<br>极小的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even a minute trace of the chemical can spoil the batch.  即使极微量的这种化学品也会毁掉整批产品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paltry</td><td>/ˈpɔːltri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无价值的；微不足道的</td><td>a paltry sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered a paltry sum for a painting worth thousands of dollars.  他们为一幅价值数千美元的画只出了微不足道的一笔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peanut</td><td>/ˈpiːnət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>微不足道的小钱；花生</td><td>work for peanuts<br>拿极少的钱干活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They paid him peanuts for a whole month of hard work.  他辛苦干了整整一个月，只拿到极少的钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 petty</td><td>/ˈpeti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>petty complaints<br>琐碎的抱怨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wasted the meeting on petty complaints about parking spaces.  他把会议浪费在关于停车位的琐碎抱怨上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trifling</td><td>/ˈtraɪflɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>a trifling sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the missing coins as a trifling sum not worth reporting.  他把丢失的硬币当作微不足道的小钱，不值得上报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trivial</td><td>/ˈtrɪviəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无足轻重的，不重要的</td><td>a trivial detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor refused to argue over such a trivial difference in wording.  编辑不愿为措辞上如此无足轻重的差别争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 big</td><td>/ˈbɪɡ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，大幅的；重要的</td><td>a big salary increase<br>大幅加薪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was offered a big raise and took the job at once.  她获得大幅加薪，立刻接受了这份工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consequential</td><td>/ˌkɑːnsəˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；随之而来的</td><td>a consequential ruling<br>一项重要的裁决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That quiet vote proved the most consequential act of the session.  那次不起眼的投票成了本届会议最重要的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 considerable</td><td>/kənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（数量上）可观（而值得注意）的</td><td>a considerable sum<br>一笔可观的钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The renovation required a considerable amount of money and nearly two years of work.  这次翻修耗费了可观的资金和近两年的工期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 significant</td><td>/səɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；显著的</td><td>a significant difference<br>显著的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers found a significant drop in the pollution levels.  研究者发现污染水平显著下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>measured</b></td><td>/ˈmeʒərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的，审慎的</td><td>a measured response<br>审慎的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister gave a measured response that neither denied nor confirmed the accusations.  部长做出了审慎的回应，既未否认也未证实这些指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 advised</td><td>/ədˈvaɪzd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>经过考虑的，慎重的</td><td>a well advised decision<br>深思后的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Postponing the launch was a well advised decision after all.  推迟发布终究是个经过慎重考虑的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 calculated</td><td>/ˈkælkjuleɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>经过深思熟虑的，有意的</td><td>a calculated risk<br>经过权衡的风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investing now was a calculated risk that finally paid off.  现在投资是经过盘算的风险，最终有了回报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 considered</td><td>/kənˈsɪdərd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>经过深思的，慎重的</td><td>a considered opinion<br>深思后的意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That is my considered judgment after reading the whole report.  这是我读完整份报告后的慎重判断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 knowing</td><td>/ˈnoʊɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>故意的，明知的</td><td>a knowing violation of the rules<br>明知故犯的违规</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge called it a knowing violation of the safety rules.  法官称这是明知故犯地违反安全规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reasoned</td><td>/ˈriːzənd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>经过推理的，理由充分的</td><td>a reasoned argument<br>有理有据的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She presented a reasoned case for keeping the library open.  她提出了有理有据的理由要求图书馆继续开放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 studied</td><td>/ˈstʌdid/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的；刻意的</td><td>studied indifference<br>刻意装出的冷淡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered with studied calm that impressed the whole panel.  他以刻意保持的镇定作答，让整个评审组印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thoughtful</td><td>/ˈθɔːtfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的；体贴的</td><td>a thoughtful measured reply<br>深思熟虑的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a thoughtful reply after considering the question carefully.  她仔细考虑问题后给出深思熟虑的回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weighed</td><td>/ˈweɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>斟酌过的，慎重的</td><td>carefully weighed words<br>字字斟酌的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every sentence in the letter was carefully weighed.  信中每句话都经过仔细斟酌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 casual</td><td>/ˈkæʒəwəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>随意的，非故意的</td><td>a casual remark<br>随口的一句话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A casual remark at dinner accidentally revealed the whole secret.  晚餐时随口一句话意外揭出了整个秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>meddle</b></td><td>/ˈmedl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>干涉，管闲事</td><td>meddle in her affairs<br>干涉她的私事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors should not meddle in the private affairs of the family next door.  邻居不该干涉隔壁一家的私事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 interfere</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈfɪr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>干涉，介入；妨碍</td><td>interfere in family matters<br>干涉家庭事务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please do not interfere in decisions that are not yours.  请不要干涉不属于你的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 interlope</del></td><td>/ˌɪntərˈloʊp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>闯入，多管闲事</td><td>interlope in a private talk<br>闯入私下的谈话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He interloped in the negotiation without any invitation at all.  他毫无邀请就插进了谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 intermeddle</del></td><td>/ˌɪntərˈmɛdəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>干涉，插手</td><td>intermeddle in local politics<br>插手地方政治</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Foreign advisers intermeddled in the country's internal affairs.  外国顾问插手了该国内政。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intrude</td><td>/ɪnˈtruːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>侵入，打扰</td><td>intrude on her privacy<br>侵犯她的隐私</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I hope I am not intruding on your quiet evening.  希望我没有打扰你安静的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 obtrude</del></td><td>/əbˈtruːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>强加，硬挤入；侵扰</td><td>obtrude one's opinions<br>强行发表意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He obtruded his advice on everyone at the table.  他向桌上每个人强行灌输自己的建议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 poke</td><td>/ˈpoʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>缓慢行进，闲荡</td><td>poke along the road<br>沿路慢慢晃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old truck poked along the highway at thirty miles an hour.  那辆旧卡车以每小时三十英里在公路上慢慢晃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pry</td><td>/praɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>刺探，打听</td><td>pry into his private life<br>打探他的私生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters continued to pry into his private life for many weeks.  记者们连续数周不断打探他的私生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snoop</td><td>/ˈsnuːp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>窥探，探听</td><td>snoop around the office<br>在办公室四处窥探</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone had been snooping around my desk all week.  整整一周都有人在我桌子附近窥探。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disregard</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>漠视，不关注；缺乏兴趣，缺乏关心</td><td>total disregard for safety<br>完全不顾安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their total disregard for safety rules eventually caused a serious accident.  他们完全不顾安全规定，最终造成了一起严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ignore</td><td>/ˌɪɡˈnɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视，不理</td><td>ignore the warning<br>忽视警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain ignored the warning and sailed into the storm.  船长忽视了警告，驶入了风暴之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 neglect</td><td>/nəˈɡlekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>忽视，疏忽；忽略，怠慢</td><td>neglect his duties<br>疏忽职责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He neglected his duties for weeks and was finally dismissed.  他数周疏忽职责，最终被解雇了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 omit</td><td>/oʊˈmɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>省略，遗漏，忽略</td><td>omit the details<br>省略细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The summary omits the details that made his argument convincing.  摘要省略了使他论证有说服力的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 overlook</td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌlʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视；宽恕；俯视</td><td>overlook a minor error<br>宽容小错</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher chose to overlook the boy's late arrival.  老师决定不追究那男孩的迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mediate</b></td><td>/ˈmiːdieɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>调解，调停</td><td>mediate a bitter dispute<br>调解一场激烈争端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A retired judge agreed to mediate the bitter dispute between the two companies.  一位退休法官同意调解两家公司之间的激烈争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conciliate</td><td>/kənˈsɪlieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；调解</td><td>conciliate angry customers<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager tried to conciliate the angry customers with refunds.  经理试图用退款来安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intercede</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈsiːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>说情，代为求情</td><td>intercede on his behalf<br>替他出面说情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her teacher interceded with the principal on her behalf.  她的老师替她向校长说情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
