<tr><td>↳近 intermediate</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈmiːdiət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>中间的；中间物</td><td>an intermediate stage<br>中间阶段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reaction produces an intermediate compound that quickly breaks down again.  这个反应产生一种很快又分解的中间化合物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 interpose</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈpoʊz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>介入调停；插入</td><td>interpose in the quarrel<br>插进这场争吵调停</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A neighbor interposed before the argument turned violent.  在争吵动手之前一位邻居出面调停。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 arouse</td><td>/əˈraʊz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>唤起，激起；唤醒</td><td>arouse public suspicion<br>引起公众怀疑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The missing files aroused suspicion among the auditors.  丢失的文件引起了审计人员的怀疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 encourage</td><td>/ɪnˈkɜːrɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，激励；促进</td><td>encourage students to read<br>鼓励学生阅读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers encourage students to ask difficult questions.  好老师鼓励学生提出有难度的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 excite</td><td>/ɪkˈsaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，引发；使兴奋</td><td>excite public interest<br>激起公众兴趣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech excited strong resentment among the workers.  他的演讲激起了工人们的强烈不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foment</td><td>/foʊˈment/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>助长，煽动</td><td>foment unrest<br>煽动动乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlets were designed to foment rebellion in the provinces.  这些小册子意在煽动各省叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incite</td><td>/ɪnˈsaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>煽动，激起</td><td>incite a riot<br>煽动暴乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was charged with inciting a riot outside the county courthouse.  他被指控在县法院外煽动暴乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inflame</td><td>/ɪnˈfleɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使加剧</td><td>inflame public anger<br>使公众愤怒加剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's careless comments only inflamed public anger over the housing shortage.  市长轻率的言论只是加剧了公众对住房短缺的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 instigate</td><td>/ˈɪnstɪɡeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>煽动，激起</td><td>instigate a rebellion<br>煽动叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several officers were accused of helping to instigate the rebellion in the province.  几名军官被指控参与煽动该省的叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pique</td><td>/piːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起（兴趣）；使生气</td><td>pique her curiosity<br>激起她的好奇心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange title piqued her curiosity, so she borrowed the book.  奇怪的书名激起了她的好奇心，于是她借走了那本书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spark</td><td>/ˈspɑːrk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>引发，激起</td><td>spark a heated debate<br>引发激烈争论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark sparked a long argument at the table.  一句不慎的话在餐桌上引发了长久的争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stimulate</td><td>/ˈstɪmjəˌleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，激发；促进</td><td>stimulate economic growth<br>促进经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm rain stimulated the seeds to sprout within a week.  温暖的雨水刺激种子一周内发芽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stir</td><td>/ˈstɜːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，煽动；搅拌</td><td>stir up anger<br>激起怒火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The article stirred up anger among local residents.  这篇文章激起了当地居民的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mediocre</b></td><td>/ˌmiːdiˈoʊkər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平庸的；质量中等偏下的</td><td>a mediocre performance<br>平庸的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The orchestra's mediocre performance disappointed listeners who had waited months for the concert.  乐团平庸的表演让等了几个月的听众很失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 average</td><td>/ˈævərɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平庸的，一般的；平均数</td><td>an average student<br>成绩平平的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His work is average but it always arrives on time.  他的活儿一般，但总能按时交。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 common</td><td>/ˈkɑːmən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，常见的；共有的</td><td>a common mistake<br>常见的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Spelling errors are a common problem in student essays.  拼写错误是学生作文中常见的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commonplace</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平凡的；老生常谈</td><td>a commonplace observation<br>老生常谈的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Video calls have become commonplace in ordinary office life.  视频通话在日常办公中已很平常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fair</td><td>/ˈfer/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>公正的；相当的；晴朗的</td><td>a fair trial<br>公正的审判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone deserves a fair hearing before any decision is made.  在做出任何决定之前，人人都应得到公正的申辩机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indifferent</td><td>/ɪnˈdɪfrənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不感兴趣的，冷漠的；平庸的</td><td>indifferent to criticism<br>对批评漠不关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is entirely indifferent to what her critics say.  她对批评者说什么完全无所谓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 medium</td><td>/ˈmiːdiəm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>擅长的表现形式；媒介</td><td>her favorite medium<br>她最爱的表现媒介</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Watercolor is the medium in which he works best.  水彩是他最擅长的表现媒介。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ordinary</td><td>/ˈɔːrdəneri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普通的，平常的</td><td>just an ordinary weekday<br>只是平常的工作日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked like an ordinary commuter waiting for the morning bus.  他看上去就像个等早班公交的普通上班族。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 outstanding</td><td>/ˌaʊtˈstændɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>杰出的，突出的</td><td>an outstanding performance<br>出色的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an outstanding performance in her very first concert.  她在首场音乐会上表现出色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 excellent</td><td>/ˈeksələnt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>优秀的，极好的</td><td>excellent results<br>优异的成绩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students achieved excellent results in the final exam.  学生们在期末考试中取得了优异成绩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 exceptional</td><td>/ɪkˈsepʃənl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>例外的，特别的，非凡的</td><td>an exceptional talent<br>非凡的天赋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She showed exceptional talent for mathematics at a very young age.  她在很小的年纪就表现出非凡的数学天赋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extraordinary</td><td>/ɪkˈstrɔːrdəneri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非凡的，特别的</td><td>extraordinary courage<br>非凡的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took extraordinary courage to report what she had seen.  举报她所见之事需要非凡的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impressive</td><td>/ɪmˈpresɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人印象深刻的</td><td>an impressive performance<br>令人印象深刻的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her command of four languages is genuinely impressive.  她掌握四门语言的能力确实令人印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 superior</td><td>/suːˈpɪriər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>更好的，占优势的；上级</td><td>superior to the rest<br>优于其余各方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This model is clearly superior to the older version.  这个型号明显优于旧版。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>medley</b></td><td>/ˈmedli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大杂烩，混合物</td><td>a medley of flavors<br>各种风味的混合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The buffet table offered a medley of flavors from six different countries.  自助餐台上供应着来自六个不同国家的各式风味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agglomerate</td><td>/əˈɡlɑːməreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>结块，聚成一团</td><td>agglomerate into lumps<br>聚结成块</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wet powder agglomerated into hard grey lumps.  湿粉结成了灰色的硬块。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 collage</td><td>/kəˈlɑːʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>拼贴画，混合物</td><td>a collage of photographs<br>照片拼贴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her film is a collage of interviews and old home movies.  她的影片是访谈与旧家庭录像的拼贴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hodgepodge</td><td>/ˈhɑːdʒpɑːdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>大杂烩</td><td>a hodgepodge of styles<br>风格的大杂烩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The house is a hodgepodge of every style he admired.  这房子是他欣赏过的各种风格的大杂烩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jumble</td><td>/ˈdʒʌmbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>混乱，杂乱堆积；使混乱</td><td>a jumble of ideas<br>杂乱的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her drawer held a jumble of receipts, coins, and old keys.  她的抽屉里杂乱地堆着收据、硬币和旧钥匙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 montage</td><td>/mɑːnˈtɑːʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大杂烩</td><td>a montage of images<br>图像的拼贴组合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exhibit opened with a montage of images from a century of protest.  展览以一组来自百年抗争的图像拼贴开场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 motley</td><td>/ˈmɑːtli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>混杂的，富于变化的</td><td>a motley crowd<br>混杂的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A motley crowd of farmers, students, and shopkeepers filled the town square.  农民、学生和店主组成的混杂人群挤满了市镇广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muddle</td><td>/ˈmʌdəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使混乱，弄糟；混乱</td><td>muddle the facts<br>把事实弄乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too many footnotes muddled the argument of his short essay.  过多脚注把他短文的论证弄乱了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 salad</td><td>/ˈsæləd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大杂烩，混合物；色拉</td><td>a salad of styles<br>风格的大杂烩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The album is a salad of jazz, rock and folk tunes.  这张专辑是爵士、摇滚和民谣的大杂烩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shuffle</td><td>/ˈʃʌfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>混杂，打乱；洗牌</td><td>a shuffle of old photographs<br>一堆打乱的旧照片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He shuffled the papers into a hopeless mixture.  他把文件混成了一团乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 variety</td><td>/vəˈraɪəti/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>多样，种类繁多</td><td>a wide variety of dishes<br>各式各样的菜肴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market offers a wide variety of local cheeses.  市场上有各式各样的本地奶酪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 welter</td><td>/ˈweltər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>混乱，动乱</td><td>a welter of details<br>杂乱的一堆细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report buried its main point in a welter of small details.  这份报告把要点埋在了杂乱的琐碎细节之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>meek</b></td><td>/miːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的</td><td>a meek and quiet manner<br>温顺安静的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too meek to complain, he accepted the extra work without saying a word.  他太顺从了，不敢抱怨，一句话没说就接下了额外的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compliant</td><td>/kəmˈplaɪənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的</td><td>a compliant employee<br>顺从的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children were unusually compliant during the long ceremony.  在漫长的仪式上，孩子们出奇地听话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 docile</td><td>/ˈdɑːsəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>温顺的，听话的</td><td>a docile animal<br>温顺的动物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old horse was docile enough for beginners to ride.  这匹老马很温顺，初学者也能骑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 manageable</td><td>/ˈmænɪdʒəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易管理的，好对付的</td><td>a manageable workload<br>可应付的工作量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Split the task into smaller, manageable pieces first.  先把任务拆成较小、好处理的部分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obedient</td><td>/əˈbiːdiənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>服从的，顺从的</td><td>an obedient child<br>听话的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog sat quietly, obedient to every hand signal.  那只狗安静地坐着，服从每个手势。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 submissive</td><td>/səbˈmɪsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>服从的，顺从的</td><td>a submissive attitude<br>顺从的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a submissive tone that surprised her colleagues.  她以顺从的语气说话，令同事惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tractable</td><td>/ˈtræktəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易驾驭的，温顺的</td><td>a tractable student<br>听话的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puppy proved surprisingly tractable during the first weeks of training.  这只小狗在最初几周的训练中出乎意料地听话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adamant</td><td>/ˈædəmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，不可动摇的</td><td>adamant about the rules<br>对规则毫不通融</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach was adamant that no player would skip practice.  教练坚决要求没有球员可以缺席训练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 headstrong</td><td>/ˈhedˌstrɔːŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>任性的，固执的</td><td>a headstrong child<br>任性的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The headstrong young man ignored every warning from his parents.  这个任性的年轻人对父母的每句劝告都置之不理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intractable</td><td>/ˌɪnˈtræktəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难对付的，棘手的</td><td>an intractable problem<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poverty remains an intractable problem in many rural regions.  贫困在许多农村地区仍是棘手的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obstinate</td><td>/ˈɑːbstɪnət/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的</td><td>an obstinate refusal<br>固执的拒绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her obstinate refusal to compromise delayed the agreement by several months.  她固执地拒绝妥协，使协议推迟了几个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 refractory</td><td>/rɪˈfræktəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>倔强的，不顺从的</td><td>a refractory pupil<br>不顺从的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One refractory pupil disrupted the lesson for the entire class.  一个不顺从的学生扰乱了全班的课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stubborn</td><td>/ˈstʌbərn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，顽固的</td><td>as stubborn as a mule<br>像骡子一样固执</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My grandfather was too stubborn to see a doctor.  我祖父太固执，不肯去看医生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unruly</td><td>/ʌnˈruːli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难驾驭的，不守规矩的</td><td>an unruly crowd<br>不守规矩的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two teachers struggled to calm the unruly class.  两位老师费力地安抚那个不守规矩的班级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unyielding</td><td>/ʌnˈjiːldɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不屈的，不让步的</td><td>unyielding opposition<br>毫不让步的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union remained unyielding on the question of wages.  工会在工资问题上依然毫不让步。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>meet</b></td><td>/miːt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合适的</td><td>a meet response<br>合适的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A public apology seemed the only meet response to such a serious blunder.  公开道歉似乎是对如此严重失误唯一合适的回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 applicable</td><td>/ˈæplɪkəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可用的，可行的；相关的，有关的</td><td>applicable to all students<br>适用于所有学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This rule is applicable to all students regardless of their year.  这条规定适用于所有学生，不论他们是哪个年级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appropriate</td><td>/əˈproʊprieɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>私自挪用；适当的</td><td>appropriate public funds<br>挪用公款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials were accused of appropriating public money for private use.  官员们被指控将公款挪为私用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apt</td><td>/æpt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恰当的，合适的；聪明的</td><td>an apt description<br>恰当的描述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That is an apt description of how the meeting actually went.  那是对会议实际情况的一个恰当描述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 becoming</td><td>/bɪˈkʌmɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>合适的，得体的</td><td>a becoming dress<br>得体的裙子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her modest reply was becoming in such a formal setting.  在如此正式的场合，她谦逊的回答很得体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 befitting</td><td>/bɪˈfɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>适当的，相称的</td><td>a welcome befitting a hero<br>配得上英雄的欢迎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They gave him a farewell befitting a long and honest career.  他们为他举行了与漫长诚实的职业生涯相称的告别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 felicitous</td><td>/fɪˈlɪsətəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恰当的，贴切的</td><td>a felicitous phrase<br>贴切的措辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The title is a felicitous choice for such a book.  这个书名对这样一本书来说是贴切的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fitting</td><td>/ˈfɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>合适的，恰当的</td><td>a fitting tribute<br>恰当的致敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The quiet ceremony was a fitting end to his career.  这场安静的仪式为他的职业生涯做了恰当的收尾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proper</td><td>/ˈprɑːpər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>适当的，正确的</td><td>the proper procedure<br>正确的程序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please store the samples in the proper containers.  请把样本存放在合适的容器里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 right</td><td>/ˈraɪt/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词、名词</td><td>正确的；正常的，妥当的</td><td>put things right<br>把事情弄妥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You were right about the weather turning cold tonight.  你说今晚天气会变冷，说对了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 suitable</td><td>/ˈsuːtəbəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合适的，适宜的</td><td>suitable for beginners<br>适合初学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This film is not suitable for very young children.  这部影片不适合幼儿观看。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 improper</td><td>/ˌɪmˈprɑːpər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适当的，不合礼节的</td><td>improper use of funds<br>资金使用不当</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was dismissed for improper conduct toward a colleague.  他因对同事行为不当而被解雇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inapplicable</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈplɪkəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适用的，不相关的</td><td>inapplicable to this case<br>不适用于本案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old rule is inapplicable to digital contracts.  旧规则不适用于电子合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inapposite</del></td><td>/ɪnˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不恰当的，不相关的</td><td>an inapposite comparison<br>不恰当的比较</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comparison to wartime rationing seems inapposite today.  把它比作战时配给在今天显得不恰当。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inappropriate</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈproʊpriət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适当的，不合适的</td><td>inappropriate behavior at work<br>工作中的不当行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wearing shorts to the interview was clearly inappropriate.  穿短裤去面试显然不合适。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inapt</td><td>/ɪnˈæpt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不恰当的；不熟练的</td><td>an inapt analogy<br>不恰当的类比</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His inapt remarks embarrassed everyone at the dinner table.  他不得体的言论让餐桌上的人都很尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 infelicitous</del></td><td>/ɪnfɪˈlɪsɪtəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不愉快的，不幸的；不适当的，不合时宜的</td><td>an infelicitous remark<br>不合时宜的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His infelicitous remark about her family ruined the otherwise pleasant dinner party.  他关于她家庭的不合时宜的话毁了本来愉快的晚宴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 malapropos</del></td><td>/ˌmæləprəˈpoʊ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>不合时宜的</td><td>a malapropos joke<br>不合时宜的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His malapropos comment about money embarrassed everyone at dinner.  他关于钱的不合时宜的评论让餐桌上所有人尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mellifluous</b></td><td>/meˈlɪfluəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（声音）甜美流畅的</td><td>a mellifluous voice<br>甜美悦耳的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The narrator's mellifluous voice made even the dullest chapters pleasant to listen to.  叙述者优美悦耳的嗓音让最枯燥的章节也变得动听。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dulcet</td><td>/ˈdʌlsɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悦耳的，令人愉悦的</td><td>dulcet tones<br>悦耳的音调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her dulcet tones calmed the restless children within a couple of minutes.  她悦耳的音调几分钟内就安抚了不安的孩子们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 euphonious</td><td>/juːˈfoʊniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悦耳的</td><td>a euphonious name<br>悦耳的名字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet chose euphonious words that pleased the ear when read aloud.  诗人选用朗读时悦耳的词语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 mellifluent</del></td><td>/mɛˈlɪfluənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>声音甜美流畅的</td><td>a mellifluent singing voice<br>甜美流畅的歌声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her mellifluent voice made the old poem sound completely new.  她甜美流畅的嗓音让这首老诗听起来全然新鲜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mellow</td><td>/ˈmeloʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>温和的，圆熟的；变温和</td><td>grow mellow with age<br>随年岁渐趋温和</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has grown much mellower since he became a grandfather.  当上祖父后他变得温和多了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 melodious</td><td>/məˈloʊdiəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悦耳的，旋律优美的</td><td>a melodious voice<br>悦耳的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her melodious laugh carried across the courtyard on the still evening.  她悦耳的笑声在静夜里穿过庭院。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cacophonous</td><td>/kəˈkɑfənəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺耳的，不和谐的</td><td>a cacophonous racket<br>刺耳的喧闹声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The orchestra produced a cacophonous din while tuning up.  乐队调音时发出刺耳的嘈杂声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 raspy</td><td>/ˈræspi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>声音刺耳的</td><td>a raspy voice<br>沙哑刺耳的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of smoking had left him with a low, raspy voice.  多年吸烟使他的嗓音低沉而刺耳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>melodramatic</b></td><td>/ˌmelədrəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸张做作的，煽情的</td><td>a melodramatic reaction<br>夸张的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her melodramatic sighs made the argument seem almost funny.  她夸张的叹气让这场争吵显得有点好笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dramatic</td><td>/drəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>戏剧性的；夸张的；显著的</td><td>a dramatic improvement<br>显著的改善</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her dramatic gestures made the simple announcement seem like a tragedy.  她夸张的手势让这个简单的通知显得像一场悲剧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hammy</td><td>/ˈhæmi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表演过火的，做作的</td><td>a hammy performance<br>过火做作的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film is ruined by hammy acting in the last scene.  最后一场戏表演过火，毁了整部片子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 histrionic</td><td>/ˌhɪstriˈɑːnɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸张做作的</td><td>histrionic gestures<br>夸张做作的手势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her histrionic response to the small mistake embarrassed the whole team.  她对这个小错误夸张做作的反应让全队难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mannered</td><td>/ˈmænərd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不自然的，做作的</td><td>a mannered writing style<br>做作的写作风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her mannered prose impressed critics at first but soon began to feel tiresome.  她做作的文风起初让评论家赞叹，但很快就令人厌倦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretentious</td><td>/prɪˈtenʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的，自命不凡的</td><td>pretentious prose<br>浮夸的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pretentious speech annoyed almost everyone in the audience.  他浮夸的演讲让几乎所有听众反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 stagy</del></td><td>/ˈsteɪdʒi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>做作的，戏剧化的</td><td>a stagy performance<br>做作的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His stagy bow at the door annoyed the other guests.  他在门口做作的鞠躬让其他客人反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nondramatic</del></td><td>/ˌnɑndrəˈmætɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非戏剧的，不夸张的</td><td>a nondramatic reading style<br>不带戏剧腔的朗读方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He prefers a plain, nondramatic way of telling stories.  他偏爱平实、不夸张的讲故事方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nontheatrical</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnθiˈætrɪkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非戏剧化的，不夸张的</td><td>a nontheatrical delivery<br>不夸张的表达方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her nontheatrical delivery made the whole speech feel honest.  她不夸张的表达让整篇讲话显得真诚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>menace</b></td><td>/ˈmenəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>威胁，使处于危险</td><td>menace world peace<br>威胁世界和平</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rising sea levels menace coastal villages that have stood for hundreds of years.  海平面上升威胁着屹立数百年的沿海村庄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compromise</td><td>/ˈkɑːmprəmaɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>妥协，折中；危害</td><td>reach a compromise<br>达成妥协</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After long debate the committee reached a workable compromise.  经过长时间辩论，委员会达成了可行的妥协。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hazard</td><td>/ˈhæzərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>危险，危害；使遭危险</td><td>a fire hazard<br>火灾隐患</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loose wiring in the basement is a serious fire hazard.  地下室松脱的电线是严重的火灾隐患。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperil</td><td>/ˌɪmˈperəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使处于危险，危及</td><td>imperil public safety<br>危及公共安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cutting the inspections would imperil the safety of every passenger.  削减检查会危及每位乘客的安全。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jeopardize</td><td>/ˈdʒepərˌdaɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>危及，损害</td><td>jeopardize the deal<br>危及这笔交易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark could jeopardize years of patient diplomacy.  一句不慎的话可能危及多年耐心的外交努力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peril</td><td>/ˈperəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>危险，风险</td><td>at your peril<br>冒着危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old bridge put every crossing traveler in real peril.  那座旧桥让每个通行的旅人都身处险境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 risk</td><td>/ˈrɪsk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>风险；冒…的危险</td><td>take a big risk<br>承担很大风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He risked his savings on a completely new business.  他拿积蓄冒险投入一项全新的生意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 threaten</td><td>/ˈθretən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>威胁，恐吓；预示危险</td><td>threaten legal action<br>威胁采取法律行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company threatened to sue anyone who leaked the documents.  公司威胁要起诉任何泄露文件的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rescue</td><td>/ˈreskju/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>解救，营救</td><td>rescue the trapped miners<br>营救被困矿工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Helicopters rescued twelve people from the flooded village.  直升机从被淹的村庄救出十二人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 save</td><td>/ˈseɪv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拯救，挽救；节省</td><td>save him from danger<br>把他从危险中救出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lifeguard saved two swimmers from the strong current.  救生员从急流中救出两名游泳者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 9

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mendacious</b></td><td>/menˈdeɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>撒谎的，虚假的</td><td>a mendacious account<br>虚假的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His mendacious account of the accident collapsed under a few simple questions.  他对事故的虚假陈述在几个简单问题下就站不住脚了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dishonest</td><td>/dɪˈsɑːnəst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不诚实的，欺骗的</td><td>a dishonest business practice<br>不诚实的商业做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company used dishonest advertising to attract careless customers.  公司用不诚实的广告吸引不细心的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deceitful</td><td>/dəˈsiːtfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欺诈的，骗人的</td><td>deceitful practices<br>欺诈的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The firm was fined for deceitful advertising practices.  该公司因欺诈性广告做法被罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fallacious</td><td>/fəˈleɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谬误的，不合逻辑的</td><td>a fallacious argument<br>谬误的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer exposed the fallacious reasoning behind the entire claim.  律师揭穿了整个主张背后的谬误推理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lying</td><td>/ˈlaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>撒谎的；说谎</td><td>a lying witness<br>撒谎的证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He called the report lying propaganda and walked out.  他称那份报告是撒谎的宣传，然后走了出去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spurious</td><td>/ˈspjʊriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假的，伪造的</td><td>spurious claims<br>虚假的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The website spread spurious claims about the new vaccine.  该网站传播关于新疫苗的虚假说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 untruthful</td><td>/ʌnˈtruːθfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不诚实的，虚假的</td><td>an untruthful answer<br>不实的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her untruthful answers soon caught the officer's attention.  她不实的回答很快引起了警官的注意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 authentic</td><td>/ɔːˈθentɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实的，非仿造的</td><td>authentic Italian cuisine<br>正宗的意大利菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum confirmed the letter was an authentic original.  博物馆确认这封信是真实的原件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honest</td><td>/ˈɑːnəst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>诚实的，正直的</td><td>an honest answer<br>一个诚实的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Give me an honest answer, even if it disappoints me.  给我一个诚实的答复，即使会让我失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 truthful</td><td>/ˈtruːθfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>诚实的，如实的</td><td>a truthful answer<br>一个诚实的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Give me a truthful answer about where you were.  告诉我你当时在哪里，要说实话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 veracious</del></td><td>/vəˈreɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实准确的；诚实的</td><td>a veracious witness<br>诚实的证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court relied on one veracious witness to settle the case.  法庭依靠一位诚实的证人来了结此案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mentor</b></td><td>/ˈmentɔːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>教导，指导</td><td>mentor young engineers<br>指导年轻工程师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Senior researchers mentor young engineers throughout their first two years at the laboratory.  资深研究员在年轻工程师入职头两年里全程指导他们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coach</td><td>/ˈkoʊtʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>指导，训练；教练</td><td>coach a new employee<br>指导新员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She coached him through his difficult first month at work.  她指导他度过了工作中艰难的第一个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 counsel</td><td>/ˈkaʊnsəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>建议，劝告</td><td>counsel patience<br>劝人耐心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lawyer counseled him to say nothing to the press.  他的律师建议他对媒体什么都别说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lead</td><td>/liːd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>引导，带领</td><td>lead a team of interns<br>带领一队实习生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He leads new employees through every step of the process.  他带着新员工走完流程的每一步。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pilot</td><td>/ˈpaɪlət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>试验性的，初步的；试行</td><td>a pilot program<br>试点项目</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school will pilot the new reading program in three classes.  学校将在三个班级试行这个新的阅读项目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shepherd</td><td>/ˈʃepərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>引领，带领照看</td><td>shepherd students through the exam<br>带领学生度过考试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher shepherded the children safely across the road.  老师把孩子们安全地领过马路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 show</td><td>/ˈʃoʊ/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>显示，展现；展览</td><td>show your feelings<br>表露感情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rarely shows his feelings, even to close friends.  他很少表露感情，连对好友也如此。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tutor</td><td>/ˈtuːtər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>辅导；导师，家教</td><td>tutor students in math<br>辅导学生数学</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tutors two neighborhood children in reading after school.  他放学后辅导两个邻家孩子阅读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comply</td><td>/kəmˈplaɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>遵从，服从</td><td>comply with the rules<br>遵守规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All drivers must comply with the new speed limits.  所有司机都必须遵守新的限速规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 follow</td><td>/ˈfɑːloʊ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>遵循，跟随</td><td>follow the rules<br>遵守规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 If you follow the instructions exactly, nothing can go wrong.  只要严格按说明操作，就不会出错。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 observe</td><td>/əbˈzɜːrv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>遵守；观察</td><td>observe the law<br>遵守法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All visitors are expected to observe the museum's rules.  所有参观者都应遵守博物馆的规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mercenary</b></td><td>/ˈmɜːrsəneri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>唯利是图的，贪婪的</td><td>mercenary motives<br>唯利是图的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her mercenary motives became obvious once the inheritance was finally mentioned.  一提到遗产，她唯利是图的动机就显露无遗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acquisitive</td><td>/əˈkwɪzətɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贪婪的</td><td>an acquisitive society<br>物欲横流的社会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The acquisitive collector bought every painting that came onto the market.  这位贪得无厌的收藏家买下了市面上出现的每一幅画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avaricious</td><td>/ˌævəˈrɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贪财的，贪婪的</td><td>an avaricious landlord<br>贪财的房东</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The avaricious banker cared for nothing but the size of his bonus.  这个贪财的银行家只在乎奖金的多少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avid</td><td>/ˈævɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>急切渴望的，热衷的</td><td>an avid reader<br>热衷阅读的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My grandmother is an avid gardener even in winter.  我祖母即使在冬天也是个热衷园艺的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 covetous</td><td>/ˈkʌvətəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贪婪的，渴求财富的</td><td>covetous of his wealth<br>贪求他的财富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cast a covetous eye on his neighbor's newly bought tractor.  他贪婪地盯着邻居新买的拖拉机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grasping</td><td>/ˈɡræspɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贪婪的，唯利是图的</td><td>a grasping moneylender<br>唯利是图的放贷人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His grasping relatives fought over the estate before the funeral ended.  他贪婪的亲戚在葬礼结束前就争夺遗产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 greedy</td><td>/ˈɡriːdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贪婪的，贪心的</td><td>greedy for money<br>贪财</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He grew greedy and lost everything in one bad deal.  他变得贪心，在一笔糟糕的交易中失去了一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 moneygrubbing</del></td><td>/ˈmʌniɡrʌbɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贪财的，唯利是图的</td><td>a moneygrubbing landlord<br>唯利是图的房东</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors called him a moneygrubbing landlord behind his back.  邻居们背后叫他唯利是图的房东。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rapacious</td><td>/rəˈpeɪʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过度贪婪的</td><td>rapacious business practices<br>贪婪的商业行径</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rapacious business practices drove several small competitors out of the market.  贪婪的商业行径把几家小竞争者挤出了市场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benevolent</td><td>/bəˈnevələnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，行善的</td><td>a benevolent employer<br>仁慈的雇主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A benevolent stranger paid for the family's train tickets.  一位仁慈的陌生人为这家人买了火车票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 generous</td><td>/ˈdʒenərəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，大量的；慷慨的</td><td>a generous helping<br>满满的一份</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She poured a generous glass of wine for each guest.  她给每位客人倒了满满一杯酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 liberal</td><td>/ˈlɪbərəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>思想前卫的；慷慨的，大方的</td><td>liberal social views<br>前卫的社会观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His liberal social views often surprised the older members of the club.  他前卫的社会观念常常让俱乐部里年长的成员感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 philanthropic</td><td>/ˌfɪlənˈθrɑːpɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>博爱的，为他人着想的</td><td>philanthropic donations<br>慈善捐赠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Philanthropic donations paid for the new hospital wing for sick children.  慈善捐赠支付了收治病童的医院新翼楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 munificent</td><td>/mjuːˈnɪfɪsnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>慷慨的</td><td>a munificent donation<br>一笔慷慨的捐赠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The university received a munificent donation from a former engineering student.  这所大学收到了一位工程系校友的慷慨捐赠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mercurial</b></td><td>/mɜːrˈkjʊriəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（情绪）善变的</td><td>a mercurial temper<br>善变的脾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His mercurial temper made colleagues hesitate before bringing him any bad news.  他善变的脾气让同事在向他报告坏消息前都要犹豫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 capricious</td><td>/kəˈprɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>反复无常的，善变的</td><td>capricious weather<br>变化无常的天气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The capricious wind kept shifting during the whole race.  整场比赛中变化无常的风一直在转向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 changeful</del></td><td>/ˈtʃeɪndʒfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>变化无常的</td><td>changeful spring weather<br>变化无常的春天天气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His changeful moods made him a hard man to work with.  他喜怒无常，很难与他共事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fluctuating</td><td>/ˈflʌktʃuˌeɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>波动的，起伏不定的</td><td>fluctuating prices<br>波动的价格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fluctuating oil prices made it hard to plan next year's budget.  波动的油价让明年的预算难以制定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fluid</td><td>/ˈfluːɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>易变的，不固定的；流体</td><td>a fluid situation<br>变动不定的局势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The schedule remains fluid until the visas finally arrive.  在签证最终到手前，日程仍不固定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mutable</td><td>/ˈmjuːtəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易变的，可变的</td><td>mutable rules<br>可变的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fashion is mutable, but good tailoring never goes away.  时尚易变，但好裁剪永不过时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 temperamental</td><td>/ˌtemprəˈmentəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喜怒无常的；不稳定的</td><td>a temperamental artist<br>喜怒无常的艺术家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old printer is temperamental on humid summer days.  这台旧打印机在潮湿的夏日很不稳定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncertain</td><td>/ʌnˈsɜːrtn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不确定的，不明朗的</td><td>an uncertain future<br>不明朗的未来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The date of the meeting remains uncertain until funding is approved.  在资金获批前会议日期仍不确定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 variable</td><td>/ˈveriəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>可变的，多变的</td><td>variable costs<br>可变成本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Winds in the strait are variable and often dangerous.  海峡里的风多变且常常危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 volatile</td><td>/ˈvɑːlətl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多变的</td><td>a volatile stock market<br>多变的股市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors hesitated because the stock market stayed volatile all spring.  由于股市整个春天都动荡多变，投资者犹豫不决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 certain</td><td>/ˈsɜːrtən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>确定的，无疑的</td><td>certain of the outcome<br>对结果有把握</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We are certain that the letter was never sent.  我们确信那封信从未寄出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 constant</td><td>/ˈkɑːnstənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不变的，恒定的；忠诚的</td><td>a constant friend<br>忠诚不变的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her support remained constant through every difficult year.  她的支持在每个艰难年头都不曾改变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 immutable</td><td>/ɪˈmjuːtəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可变的</td><td>an immutable law of nature<br>不可变的自然法则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Gravity is an immutable law that no clever argument can suspend.  引力是不可变的法则，任何巧辩都无法使它失效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 invariable</td><td>/ɪnˈveriəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不变的，恒定的</td><td>an invariable routine<br>一成不变的常规</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His morning routine has been invariable for twenty years.  他的晨间习惯二十年来一成不变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 settled</td><td>/ˈsetəld/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固定的，安定的</td><td>a settled routine<br>固定的作息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After years of moving they finally led a settled life.  多年漂泊后他们终于过上安定的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stable</td><td>/ˈsteɪbəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>稳定的；马厩</td><td>a stable income<br>稳定的收入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His condition has been stable since the operation last Tuesday.  自上周二手术以来他的病情一直稳定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 steady</td><td>/ˈstedi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稳定的，持续不变的</td><td>a steady stream of visitors<br>络绎不绝的来访者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sales have shown steady growth for eight straight quarters.  销售额已连续八个季度稳定增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unvarying</td><td>/ʌnˈvɛriɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无变化的，始终一致的</td><td>an unvarying rhythm<br>一成不变的节奏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The machine repeated the task at an unvarying speed all night.  机器整夜以一成不变的速度重复这项工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>merited</b></td><td>/ˈmerɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>应得的，理所当然的</td><td>merited praise<br>应得的赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The award was merited praise for thirty years of quiet, patient teaching.  这个奖项是对三十年默默耐心教学的应得赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 condign</del></td><td>/kənˈdaɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>应得的，恰当的</td><td>condign punishment<br>应得的惩罚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court imposed condign punishment on the corrupt inspector.  法庭对腐败的检查员施以应得的惩罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deserved</td><td>/dɪˈzɜːrvd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>应得的，理所应当的</td><td>a well deserved rest<br>应得的休息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the long tour she took a well deserved break.  长途巡演后她得到了应有的休息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 due</td><td>/ˈduː/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>应得的，应有的；到期的</td><td>give due credit<br>给予应得的赞誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The engineers never received the credit that was rightly due.  工程师们从未得到他们应得的赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fair</td><td>/ˈfer/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>公正的；相当的；晴朗的</td><td>a fair trial<br>公正的审判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone deserves a fair hearing before any decision is made.  在做出任何决定之前，人人都应得到公正的申辩机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 Justified</td><td>/ˈdʒʌstəˌfaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有正当理由的，合理的</td><td>fully justified anger<br>完全合理的愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her complaint was justified, and the store refunded the money.  她的投诉有理，商店退了钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 right</td><td>/ˈraɪt/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词、名词</td><td>正确的；正常的，妥当的</td><td>put things right<br>把事情弄妥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You were right about the weather turning cold tonight.  你说今晚天气会变冷，说对了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 warranted</td><td>/ˈwɔːrəntɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有正当理由的，应得的</td><td>fully warranted criticism<br>完全有理的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Given the delays, their anger seemed entirely warranted.  考虑到这些延误，他们的愤怒似乎完全合理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undeserved</td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈzɜːrvd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不应得的，冤枉的</td><td>undeserved blame<br>不应受的责难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The quiet clerk suffered years of undeserved blame.  那位沉默的职员多年来承受着不该受的责难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undue</td><td>/ʌnˈduː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过度的，过分的；不适当的</td><td>undue influence<br>不当的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge ruled that the will had been signed under undue influence.  法官裁定这份遗嘱是在不当影响下签署的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mesh</b></td><td>/meʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>诱捕</td><td>mesh the prey<br>捕获猎物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dolphins are sometimes meshed in nets meant for other fish.  海豚有时会被捕捉其他鱼类的渔网缠住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 enmesh</del></td><td>/ɪnˈmeʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使卷入，使陷入</td><td>enmesh in a scandal<br>使卷入丑闻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He became enmeshed in a lawsuit that lasted a decade.  他被卷入一场持续十年的诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ensnare</td><td>/ɪnˈsner/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诱捕，使落入陷阱</td><td>ensnare a wild rabbit<br>捕住一只野兔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap loans ensnared many families in years of debt.  廉价贷款使许多家庭陷入多年债务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ensnarl</del></td><td>/ɪnˈsnɑːrl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使纠缠，使陷入混乱</td><td>ensnarl the traffic downtown<br>使市中心交通陷入混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fallen crane ensnarled traffic for the entire morning.  倒下的吊车使整个上午的交通陷入混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 entoil</del></td><td>/ɛnˈtɔɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使陷入网罗，诱捕</td><td>entoil the enemy scouts<br>把敌方侦察兵诱入陷阱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clever plan entoiled the smugglers near the border.  这个巧妙的计划在边境附近诱捕了走私者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entrap</td><td>/ɪnˈtræp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诱骗</td><td>entrap an unwary buyer<br>诱骗不留神的买主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The con artist entrapped an unwary buyer with a forged certificate.  骗子用一张伪造证书诱骗了一位不留神的买主。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 net</td><td>/ˈnet/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>用网捕捉，抓住</td><td>net a butterfly<br>网住一只蝴蝶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The police netted the thief before he could reach the border.  警察在小偷逃到边境前将他抓获。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snare</td><td>/sner/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>陷阱，罗网；用陷阱捕捉</td><td>snare a rabbit<br>用套索捕兔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hunter set a snare near the stream to catch rabbits.  猎人在溪边设下套索捕兔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tangle</td><td>/ˈtæŋɡl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>纠缠，使…纠结</td><td>tangle the fishing line<br>把鱼线弄缠住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wind tangled the fishing line around the branches overhead.  风把鱼线缠在头顶的树枝上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trap</td><td>/ˈtræp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>困住，使陷入；陷阱</td><td>trap him in a lie<br>使他陷入自己的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow trapped the climbers on the ridge for days.  大雪把登山者困在山脊上好几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disentangle</td><td>/ˌdɪsɪnˈtæŋɡəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开，理清，使脱离</td><td>disentangle the wires<br>把电线理清</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took hours to disentangle fact from rumor in the report.  在这份报告中把事实与谣言分开花了好几个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 untangle</td><td>/ʌnˈtæŋɡəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开纠结，理清</td><td>untangle the knot<br>解开这个结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The auditors are still trying to untangle the company's finances.  审计人员仍在努力理清这家公司的财务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mesmeric</b></td><td>/mezˈmerɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人着迷的，难以抗拒的</td><td>a mesmeric voice<br>令人着迷的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The storyteller had a mesmeric voice that held the children completely still.  讲故事的人有种令人着迷的嗓音，让孩子们一动不动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attractive</td><td>/əˈtræktɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，迷人的</td><td>an attractive offer<br>有吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made him an attractive offer he could not refuse.  公司给了他一个无法拒绝的诱人条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alluring</td><td>/əˈlʊrɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>诱人的，有魅力的</td><td>an alluring smile<br>迷人的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The alluring aroma of fresh bread drew us into the bakery.  新鲜面包诱人的香气把我们引进了面包店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 captivating</td><td>/ˈkæptɪˌveɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，令人着迷的</td><td>a captivating performance<br>令人着迷的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her captivating voice held the entire audience in complete silence.  她迷人的歌声让全场观众鸦雀无声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 charming</td><td>/ˈtʃɑːrmɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，可爱的</td><td>a charming little village<br>迷人的小村庄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent the afternoon in a charming village by the lake.  我们在湖边一个迷人的村庄度过了下午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drawing</td><td>/ˈdrɔːɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>吸引人的；图画</td><td>a strong drawing power<br>很强的吸引力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new gallery has real drawing power among younger visitors.  这家新画廊对较年轻的参观者很有吸引力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enchanting</td><td>/ɪnˈtʃæntɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，醉人的</td><td>an enchanting melody<br>醉人的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden looked enchanting under the soft light of lanterns.  花园在灯笼柔和的光线下显得十分迷人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 riveting</td><td>/ˈrɪvɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的，极迷人的</td><td>a riveting account<br>引人入胜的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary offers a riveting account of life inside the mountain villages.  这部纪录片对山村生活的叙述引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disgusting</td><td>/dɪsˈɡʌstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a disgusting smell<br>令人恶心的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was left in a disgusting state after the party.  聚会后厨房脏得让人恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repellent</td><td>/rɪˈpelənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>令人厌恶的；驱虫剂</td><td>a repellent odor<br>令人厌恶的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I found his arrogant remarks utterly repellent and left early.  我觉得他傲慢的言论令人极其厌恶，于是提前离开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repulsive</td><td>/rɪˈpʌlsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a repulsive sight<br>令人恶心的景象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rotting fish on the beach was absolutely repulsive.  沙滩上腐烂的鱼令人极其恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>metamorphose</b></td><td>/ˌmetəˈmɔːrfoʊz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使变形，彻底改变</td><td>metamorphose into a butterfly<br>蜕变成蝴蝶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The shy student metamorphosed into a confident public speaker.  那个害羞的学生蜕变成了自信的演讲者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 alchemize</del></td><td>/ˈælkəmaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使转化，使发生变化</td><td>alchemize grief into art<br>把悲伤化为艺术</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She alchemized her hardest years into two fine novels.  她把最艰难的岁月化成了两部好小说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transfigure</td><td>/trænsˈfɪɡjər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使改变外观</td><td>transfigure her face<br>使她面容改观</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rising sunlight transfigured the plain valley into a field of gold.  初升的阳光把平凡的山谷变成一片金色的原野。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transform</td><td>/trænsˈfɔːrm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>彻底改变，转变</td><td>transform the neighborhood<br>彻底改变街区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new subway line transformed the quiet neighborhood within five years.  新地铁线五年内彻底改变了这个安静的街区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transmute</td><td>/trænzˈmjuːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使变形，使转化</td><td>transmute lead into gold<br>把铅变成黄金</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Great heat transmutes the soft metal into something far harder.  高温把这种软金属变成硬得多的东西。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transpose</td><td>/trænˈspoʊz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>调换位置，转换</td><td>transpose two letters<br>调换两个字母的位置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The typist transposed two digits in the phone number.  打字员把电话号码里的两个数字调换了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 transubstantiate</del></td><td>/ˌtrænsəbˈstænʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使实质改变，使变体</td><td>transubstantiate bread and wine<br>使饼与酒发生变体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ritual is said to transubstantiate the bread completely.  据说这一仪式使饼彻底改变了实质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 remain</td><td>/rɪˈmeɪn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>保持不变，留存</td><td>remain unchanged for years<br>多年保持不变</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old rules remain in force despite the new law.  尽管有了新法，旧规则仍然有效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>metaphysical</b></td><td>/ˌmetəˈfɪzɪkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>形而上的，玄学的</td><td>metaphysical questions<br>形而上的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They argued about metaphysical questions until well past midnight.  他们争论形而上的问题直到深夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conceptual</td><td>/kənˈseptʃuəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>概念上的，观念的</td><td>a conceptual framework<br>概念框架</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The book offers a clear conceptual model of human memory.  这本书提供了清晰的人类记忆概念模型。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ideal</td><td>/aɪˈdiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>理想，典范；理想的</td><td>an ideal solution<br>理想的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remains the ideal of quiet courage for many students.  她仍是许多学生心中沉静勇气的典范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ideational</td><td>/ˌaɪdiˈeɪʃənəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>观念的，概念形成的</td><td>ideational content<br>观念性的内容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The therapy focuses on ideational patterns behind constant anxiety.  这种疗法关注持续焦虑背后的观念模式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 notional</td><td>/ˈnoʊʃənəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>概念上的，想象的</td><td>a notional boundary<br>假想的界线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The line on the map is purely notional, not a real fence.  地图上这条线纯属假想，并非真的围栏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 theoretical</td><td>/ˌθiːəˈretɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>理论的，理论上的</td><td>purely theoretical physics<br>纯理论物理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His objection is theoretical and has no bearing on daily practice.  他的反对是理论性的，与日常实践无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ethereal</td><td>/iˈθɪriəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻巧精致的；非物质的，精神（上）的</td><td>an ethereal beauty<br>轻灵飘逸的美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her voice had an ethereal quality that hushed the entire hall.  她的嗓音有种轻灵飘逸的质感，让全场安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heavenly</td><td>/ˈhevənli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天上的；美妙无比的</td><td>heavenly bodies<br>天体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Astronomers charted the heavenly bodies visible that clear night.  天文学家绘制了那个晴夜可见的天体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 otherworldly</td><td>/ˌʌðərˈwɜːrldli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>超凡脱俗的，非尘世的</td><td>an otherworldly light<br>非尘世的光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The frozen lake had an otherworldly blue glow at dawn.  黎明时结冰的湖泛着非尘世的蓝光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paranormal</td><td>/perəˈnɔːrməl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>超自然的</td><td>paranormal phenomena<br>超自然现象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She writes novels about paranormal events in quiet small towns.  她写关于宁静小镇上超自然事件的小说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preternatural</td><td>/ˌpriːtərˈnætʃrəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>超乎寻常的</td><td>preternatural calm<br>异乎寻常的平静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot showed preternatural calm while the engine was clearly failing.  发动机明显故障时，飞行员表现出超乎寻常的镇定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transcendental</td><td>/ˌtrænsenˈdentəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>超验的，超自然的</td><td>a transcendental experience<br>超验的体验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He described a transcendental moment on the mountain top.  他描述了在山顶的一次超验体验。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unearthly</td><td>/ʌnˈɜːrθli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非尘世的，诡异恐怖的</td><td>an unearthly silence<br>诡异的寂静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An unearthly cry came from somewhere deep in the forest.  森林深处传来一声诡异的叫声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unworldly</td><td>/ʌnˈwɜːrldli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>超脱世俗的，不谙世事的</td><td>an unworldly scholar<br>不谙世事的学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained unworldly, indifferent to both money and fame.  他一直超脱世俗，对金钱和名声都不在意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concrete</td><td>/ˈkɑːŋkriːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>具体的，实在的；明确的</td><td>concrete evidence<br>确凿的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The detective needed concrete evidence rather than clever theories.  侦探需要确凿的证据，而不是巧妙的推论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mundane</td><td>/mʌnˈdeɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平凡的</td><td>mundane daily chores<br>平凡的日常杂务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent the morning on mundane daily chores like laundry and dishes.  他整个上午都在做洗衣洗碗这类平凡的日常杂务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>meteoric</b></td><td>/ˌmiːtiˈɔːrɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>流星般迅速而短暂的</td><td>a meteoric rise<br>迅速而短暂的崛起</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her meteoric rise in the company ended just as suddenly as it began.  她在公司里的迅速崛起结束得和开始时一样突然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ephemeral</td><td>/ɪˈfemərəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>短暂的，转瞬即逝的</td><td>ephemeral fame<br>短暂的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Internet fame is often ephemeral and quickly forgotten.  网络名声往往短暂，很快被遗忘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evanescent</td><td>/ˌevəˈnesnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>短暂的，逐渐消失的</td><td>evanescent beauty<br>转瞬即逝的美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The evanescent glow of fireflies lit the summer path.  萤火虫短暂的微光照亮了夏夜小径。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fleeting</td><td>/ˈfliːtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>短暂的；转瞬即逝的</td><td>a fleeting moment<br>短暂的瞬间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For one fleeting moment the clouds parted above the valley.  有一个短暂的瞬间，云在山谷上方散开了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 momentary</td><td>/ˈmoʊmənˌteri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>短暂的，瞬间的</td><td>a momentary hesitation<br>片刻的犹豫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After a momentary pause she signed the contract.  短暂停顿之后她签了合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rapid</td><td>/ˈræpəd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速的，快速的</td><td>rapid economic growth<br>迅速的经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city saw rapid change after the railway finally arrived.  铁路终于通达后这座城市迅速变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transient</td><td>/ˈtrænʃənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>短暂的；暂时的；过往的</td><td>transient population<br>流动人口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fame in the internet age is often transient and quickly forgotten.  网络时代的名声往往短暂，很快被人遗忘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transitory</td><td>/ˈtrænsətɔːri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>短暂的</td><td>transitory pleasure<br>短暂的快乐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His anger was transitory and he apologized within the hour.  他的愤怒很短暂，一小时内就道了歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enduring</td><td>/ɪnˈdʊrɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>持久的，长存的</td><td>an enduring friendship<br>长久的友谊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their enduring friendship survived decades of separation.  他们持久的友谊经历了数十年的分离。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lasting</td><td>/ˈlæstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>持久的，永久的</td><td>a lasting impression<br>持久的印象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her kindness left a lasting impression on everyone in the ward.  她的善意给病房里每个人留下持久印象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 permanent</td><td>/ˈpɜːrmənənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>永恒的</td><td>a permanent solution<br>永久的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They are seeking a permanent solution rather than another quick fix.  他们在寻找永久的解决办法，而不是又一个权宜之计。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prolonged</td><td>/prəˈlɔːŋd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>延长的，持久的</td><td>a prolonged drought<br>持续很久的旱情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The region suffered a prolonged drought lasting three years.  该地区遭受了长达三年的旱灾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 10

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>methodical</b></td><td>/məˈθɑːdɪkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>井然有序的，有条理的</td><td>a methodical approach<br>有条理的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A methodical search of the archive finally produced the missing land records.  对档案的有条理搜查终于找出了缺失的土地记录。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 neat</td><td>/ˈniːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>整洁的，井然有序的</td><td>neat handwriting<br>整洁的字迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her neat handwriting made the notes easy to read.  她整洁的字迹让笔记很好读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 orderly</td><td>/ˈɔːrdərli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有秩序的，整齐的</td><td>an orderly queue<br>整齐的队伍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students left the hall in an orderly line.  学生们排成整齐的队伍离开礼堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 organized</td><td>/ˈɔːrɡəˌnaɪzd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有条理的，有组织的</td><td>highly organized files<br>条理分明的文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She keeps an organized notebook for every course she takes.  她为每门课都备有一本条理分明的笔记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 regular</td><td>/ˈreɡjələr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>规律的，通常的</td><td>on a regular basis<br>定期地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He goes swimming on a regular basis every week.  他每周都定期去游泳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 systematic</td><td>/ˌsɪstəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>系统的，有条理的</td><td>a systematic review<br>系统性回顾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team took a systematic approach to checking each component.  团队用系统的方法逐一检查了每个部件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 systematized</td><td>/ˈsɪstəmətaɪzd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>系统化的，有条理的</td><td>a systematized filing method<br>系统化的归档方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her notes were carefully systematized by subject and by date.  她的笔记按主题和日期作了系统整理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disorganized</td><td>/dɪsˈɔːrɡənaɪzd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>杂乱无章的，没条理的</td><td>a disorganized desk<br>乱七八糟的书桌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project failed because the team was hopelessly disorganized.  项目失败是因为团队毫无条理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 haphazard</td><td>/hæpˈhæzərd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>随意的；杂乱无章的</td><td>a haphazard manner<br>杂乱无章的方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The files were stacked in a haphazard pile on the office floor.  文件杂乱无章地堆在办公室的地板上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 irregular</td><td>/ɪˈreɡjələr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不规则的，不平整的</td><td>an irregular shape<br>不规则的形状</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The path was made of irregular stones of many sizes.  这条小路由各种大小不规则的石头铺成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unsystematic</td><td>/ˌʌnsɪstəˈmætɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无系统的，杂乱的</td><td>an unsystematic search<br>毫无条理的搜寻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their unsystematic reading of the data missed the key trend.  他们杂乱地读数据，漏掉了关键趋势。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>meticulous</b></td><td>/məˈtɪkjələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>一丝不苟的，极为细致的</td><td>meticulous attention to detail<br>对细节极为在意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The restorer's meticulous attention to detail saved the fragile manuscript from further damage.  修复师一丝不苟地关注细节，使这份脆弱的手稿免于进一步损坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 careful</td><td>/ˈkerfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，仔细的</td><td>careful consideration<br>慎重考虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Be careful when you cross this busy street at night.  夜里穿过这条繁忙的街道时要小心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conscientious</td><td>/ˌkɑːnʃiˈenʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>仔细的，一丝不苟的；有良心的，正直的</td><td>a conscientious worker<br>一位认真的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A conscientious editor checks every fact before the article goes to print.  一丝不苟的编辑会在文章付印前核实每一个事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exact</td><td>/ɪɡˈzækt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>强索，征收；精确的</td><td>exact a heavy tax<br>征收重税</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The conquerors exacted tribute from every village in the valley.  征服者向谷中每个村庄强征贡赋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fussy</td><td>/ˈfʌsi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>挑剔的，过分讲究的</td><td>fussy about small details<br>对小细节很挑剔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old tailor is fussy about the smallest details.  这位老裁缝对最小的细节都很挑剔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 heedful</del></td><td>/ˈhiːdfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>留心的，注意的</td><td>heedful of the warning<br>留意警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heedful of the forecast, the climbers turned back early.  留意到天气预报，登山者提早返回了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 painstaking</td><td>/ˈpeɪnzteɪkɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>煞费苦心的，一丝不苟的</td><td>painstaking research<br>煞费苦心的研究</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of painstaking research went into this single short article.  这篇短文背后是多年一丝不苟的研究。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 punctilious</td><td>/pʌŋkˈtɪliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>注意细节的，一丝不苟的</td><td>punctilious about details<br>对细节一丝不苟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is punctilious about details that others simply ignore.  他对别人干脆忽略的细节一丝不苟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scrupulous</td><td>/ˈskruːpjələs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>正直的；一丝不苟的</td><td>scrupulous honesty<br>一丝不苟的诚实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her scrupulous honesty made her the obvious choice to handle the accounts.  她一丝不苟的诚实使她成为管账的当然人选。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 careless</td><td>/ˈkerləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗心的，疏忽的</td><td>a careless mistake<br>粗心的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark ruined months of patient negotiation.  一句不慎的话毁了数月的耐心谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 feckless</td><td>/ˈfekləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无成果的，没有价值的；粗心不负责任的</td><td>a feckless young man<br>不成事的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His feckless handling of the accounts nearly ruined the shop.  他对账目粗心不负责的处理几乎毁了这家店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 heedless</td><td>/ˈhiːdləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不注意的，不顾的</td><td>heedless of danger<br>不顾危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He ran into the street heedless of the traffic.  他不顾车流冲进了街道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 thoughtless</td><td>/ˈθɔːtləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>欠考虑的，轻率的</td><td>a thoughtless remark<br>轻率的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was thoughtless of him to slam the door at midnight.  他半夜摔门实在是欠考虑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mettle</b></td><td>/ˈmetl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇气；毅力，耐力</td><td>prove one's mettle<br>证明自己的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long climb in freezing rain proved the mettle of every young recruit.  在冻雨中的长途攀登考验出每个新兵的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bravery</td><td>/ˈbreɪvəri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇敢，英勇</td><td>an act of bravery<br>勇敢之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was given a medal for bravery during the flood.  她因在洪水中的勇敢而获得奖章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 courage</td><td>/ˈkɜːrədʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>勇气，胆量</td><td>summon up courage<br>鼓起勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took real courage to admit the mistake in front of everyone.  在众人面前承认错误需要真正的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dauntlessness</del></td><td>/ˈdɔːntləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无畏，大胆</td><td>the dauntlessness of youth<br>年轻人的无畏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His dauntlessness in the storm saved the whole crew.  他在风暴中的无畏救了全体船员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fortitude</td><td>/ˈfɔːrtɪˌtuːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>坚韧，刚毅</td><td>bear pain with fortitude<br>坚韧地忍受痛苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She bore months of treatment with quiet fortitude.  她默默坚韧地承受了数月的治疗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 guts</td><td>/ɡʌts/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇气，胆量；内脏</td><td>have the guts<br>有胆量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had the guts to quit and start her own company.  她有胆量辞职创办自己的公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nerve</td><td>/nɜːrv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>（坚强的）意志，勇气</td><td>lose one's nerve<br>失去勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He lost his nerve at the last moment and never asked the question.  他在最后一刻失去了勇气，始终没有问出那个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pluck</td><td>/plʌk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>敢于面对困难的勇气</td><td>show great pluck<br>表现出极大勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took real pluck to speak up against the whole committee.  敢于当众反对整个委员会需要真正的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spirit</td><td>/ˈspɪrət/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>勇气，志气；精神</td><td>show real spirit<br>表现出真正的骨气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The small team played with spirit until the final whistle.  这支小队斗志昂扬地打到终场哨响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spunk</td><td>/spʌŋk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇气，胆量</td><td>have the spunk to refuse<br>有胆量拒绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took real spunk to speak against her own boss.  当面反对自己的老板需要真正的胆量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 valor</td><td>/ˈvælər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>英勇，勇猛</td><td>an award for valor<br>英勇奖章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He received a medal for valor after rescuing two trapped miners.  他救出两名被困矿工后获得英勇勋章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 durability</td><td>/ˌdʊrəˈbɪləti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>耐久性，持久力</td><td>the durability of the material<br>这种材料的耐久性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Field tests proved the durability of the new leather boots.  实地测试证明了这种新皮靴的耐久性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 endurance</td><td>/ɪnˈdʊrəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>忍耐力，耐久力</td><td>physical endurance<br>身体的耐力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long runs slowly built up her endurance over the winter.  长跑在整个冬天慢慢提高了她的耐力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stamina</td><td>/ˈstæmɪnə/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>耐力</td><td>build up stamina<br>增强耐力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long morning swims helped him build up remarkable stamina for racing.  长时间的晨泳帮他练出比赛所需的惊人耐力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 persistence</td><td>/pərˈsɪstəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>坚持，持续</td><td>sheer persistence<br>全凭坚持不懈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sheer persistence, not talent, finally won her the research grant.  最终为她赢得研究资助的是坚持不懈，而非天赋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 resolution</td><td>/ˌrezəˈluːʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>决心，坚定；决议</td><td>act with resolution<br>果断坚定地行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She faced that difficult year with quiet resolution.  她以沉静的决心面对那艰难的一年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cowardice</td><td>/ˈkaʊərdɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>怯懦，胆小</td><td>an act of cowardice<br>怯懦之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refusing to speak up for a friend struck him later as cowardice.  没有为朋友说话，事后他觉得那是怯懦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 cravenness</del></td><td>/ˈkreɪvənnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>极度怯懦，胆小</td><td>sheer cravenness<br>十足的懦弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His cravenness under fire shocked the men who once admired him.  他在炮火下的懦弱令曾敬佩他的人震惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 gutlessness</del></td><td>/ˈɡʌtləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>没胆量，懦弱</td><td>political gutlessness<br>政治上的懦弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters punished the senator for his gutlessness on the crucial vote.  选民因参议员在关键投票上的懦弱而惩罚了他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 pusillanimity</del></td><td>/ˌpjuːsələˈnɪməti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>胆小，懦弱无志气</td><td>diplomatic pusillanimity<br>外交上的软弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their pusillanimity allowed the aggressor to seize the border towns unopposed.  他们的懦弱使侵略者不受阻碍地占领了边境城镇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 spinelessness</del></td><td>/ˈspaɪnləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>没骨气，软弱</td><td>moral spinelessness<br>道德上的软弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His spinelessness in meetings meant unfair decisions were never challenged.  他在会上没骨气，导致不公的决定从未被质疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>miff</b></td><td>/mɪf/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒</td><td>miff his colleagues<br>让同事们不快</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His careless joke about the budget miffed several colleagues at the meeting.  他关于预算的轻率玩笑让会上几位同事很不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggravate</td><td>/ˈæɡrəveɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加重，恶化；激怒</td><td>aggravate the situation<br>使局势恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Running on the injury will only aggravate it.  带着伤跑步只会让伤势加重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 annoy</td><td>/əˈnɔɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，不断烦扰</td><td>annoy the neighbors<br>不断打扰邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The barking dog began to annoy every neighbor on the quiet street.  那只不停犬吠的狗开始烦扰这条安静街上的每位邻居。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enrage</td><td>/enˈreɪdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使愤怒，激怒</td><td>enrage the audience<br>激怒观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His flippant apology only enraged the families further.  他轻率的道歉只让那些家庭更加愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exasperate</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæspəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，激怒</td><td>exasperate his parents<br>使他的父母恼火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His constant lateness exasperated even the most patient teachers.  他总是迟到，连最有耐心的老师都被激怒了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incense</td><td>/ɪnˈsens/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>incense the public<br>激怒公众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal is certain to incense feminists across the country.  这项提案肯定会激怒全国的女权主义者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infuriate</td><td>/ɪnˈfjʊrieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>infuriate the critics<br>激怒批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The decision to cut pensions infuriated thousands of workers across the country.  削减养老金的决定激怒了全国成千上万的工人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ire</td><td>/ˈaɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>愤怒，怒火</td><td>draw the ire of critics<br>引来批评者的怒火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax drew the ire of small business owners.  新税收招致了小企业主的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 madden</td><td>/ˈmædən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使发狂，使愤怒</td><td>madden the audience<br>使观众暴怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The endless delays maddened passengers waiting at the gate.  无休止的延误让在登机口等待的乘客怒不可遏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nettle</td><td>/ˈnetl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>惹怒</td><td>nettled by criticism<br>被批评惹怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was clearly nettled by the reporter's rude question.  记者无礼的提问显然惹恼了她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peeve</td><td>/piːv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打扰，惹恼</td><td>peeve the audience<br>惹恼观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud phone conversations on the train really peeve the other passengers.  火车上大声打电话确实惹恼其他乘客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vex</td><td>/veks/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使烦恼，使恼怒</td><td>vex the teacher<br>使老师恼怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant interruptions vexed the teacher throughout the long afternoon lesson.  不断的打扰让老师在漫长的下午课上十分恼怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appease</td><td>/əˈpiːz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；满足</td><td>appease the angry crowd<br>安抚愤怒的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered refunds to appease its angry customers.  公司提供退款以安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mollify</td><td>/ˈmɑːlɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>平息，抚慰，缓和</td><td>mollify an angry customer<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sincere apology from the manager mollified the furious client.  经理诚挚的道歉安抚了这位愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 placate</td><td>/ˈpleɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（通过让步以）平息抚慰</td><td>placate the critics<br>平息批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered full refunds to placate its angriest customers.  公司提供全额退款以平息最愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 propitiate</td><td>/prəˈpɪʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抚慰，劝解</td><td>propitiate the angry gods<br>抚慰愤怒的神灵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers offered rice and flowers to propitiate the mountain spirits.  村民献上米和花来抚慰山神。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>migratory</b></td><td>/ˈmaɪɡrətɔːri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迁移的</td><td>migratory birds<br>候鸟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Migratory birds pause at this shallow lake before continuing south for the winter.  候鸟在继续南飞过冬前会在这片浅湖停歇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 migrant</td><td>/ˈmaɪɡrənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>迁移的；移居者，候鸟</td><td>migrant workers<br>外来务工者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands of migrant birds rest in the marsh each spring.  每年春天数千只迁徙的鸟在这片沼泽休息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mobile</td><td>/ˈmoʊbəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>流动的，可移动的</td><td>a mobile population<br>流动人口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young workers today are far more mobile than their parents.  如今的年轻工人比父辈流动性强得多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nomad</td><td>/ˈnoʊmæd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>游牧者，居无定所的人</td><td>a desert nomad<br>沙漠中的游牧者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A desert nomad guided us across the dunes toward the hidden well.  一位沙漠游牧者带我们穿过沙丘走向隐蔽的水井。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nomadic</td><td>/noʊˈmædɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>游牧的，流浪的</td><td>a nomadic lifestyle<br>游牧的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her nomadic childhood took her through eleven countries.  她流浪的童年带她走过十一个国家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 traveling</td><td>/ˈtrævəlɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>旅行的，巡回的</td><td>a traveling circus<br>巡回马戏团</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A traveling theater group performed in the village square.  一个巡回剧团在村广场上演出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonmigrant</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈmaɪɡrənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不迁徙的；不迁徙的动物</td><td>a nonmigrant species<br>不迁徙的物种</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These nonmigrant sparrows stay in the valley all winter.  这些不迁徙的麻雀整个冬天都留在山谷里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 resident</td><td>/ˈrezɪdənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>定居的，常住的；居民</td><td>a resident population of deer<br>常住的鹿群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A resident pair of owls nests in the old stone barn.  一对定居的猫头鹰在老石谷仓里筑巢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
